The graphics settings (Settings > Video): presets Low / Medium / High / Ultra, each a full set of individual settings that the menu shows and that can be changed one by one (the preset then reads Custom; GLSettings.gfx / set_preset / matching_preset): anti-aliasing (off, FXAA, SMAA, MSAA 2x/4x/8x, TAA, MSAA 4x + TAA), render scale 50-100% with an upscaler (bilinear, FSR 1, FSR 2) and FSR sharpening, shadows (atlas 1K-8K and the soft-shadow filter quality), ambient occlusion off..ultra (SSAO and SSIL quality), global illumination (SDFGI, and its ray count), indirect light (SSIL), reflections (SSR, roughness quality), volumetric fog off..ultra (froxel volume 64-160), level of detail (mesh LOD threshold), brightness (scales each map's own grade), smooth motion, V-Sync and a frame cap (0/60/120/144/165/240/360, the shared core Settings). Ultra: MSAA 4x + TAA, 8K shadows, ultra AO/fog/LOD. Debanding is always on; anisotropic filtering is 16x (project setting, applies on launch). The map effects stay gated as before (a setting can only take away an effect the map's Environment asked for). Tests: test_gfx_settings (every preset complete, Ultra the top, a preset writes its settings and a change makes it Custom, every AA mode, the effect gates and brightness, smooth motion on by default), test_graphics_presets unchanged. Not verified in a rendered session yet: the owner's next session with the frame log on will show it.

T-134, 2026-10-29: more detail on the modelled people (the owner: "ADD MORE DETAIL TO CHARECTER MODELS"). All 17 people (squad, Jakub, the patrons, the survivors, the Cousins, Selwyn) rebuilt by tools/build_people.sh; Blender only, our own geometry, no rendered Godot window. Faces: the people's heads are built at head_res 20 instead of the zombies' 16 (people.json budget.head_res; the zombies keep 16), so the face, ears and jaw line are about 1.5x denser and never decimated; eyes are rounder (20x12), the iris finer (4 rings x 20) over a thin dark limbal ring, the pupil finer; lids and lash lines 18 segments (were 12), brows 14 (were 9). The finer head and hands no longer share the canonical topology, so their UVs map through the canonical head / hand surface (build_all._region_uvs, head knobs and hand size scaled out): the shared living skin textures still fit. Hands: budget.hand_res 1 gives the people a rounder palm and 8-sided fingers with a segment split and a knuckle swell per joint (the zombies' hands are unchanged). Hair: thicker caps (8.2 mm male, 11.8 mm female) with streaky lock noise, finer buns and braids, the bob at 60x16. Kits (make_kits.py, a finishing-detail block): pocket bags with flaps and buttons, open frame buckles with a prong and the strap's end (plain: no badge, no emblem, no symbols of any kind), belt keepers, raised welts (plackets, front edges, the centre back seam and the yoke), collar points, cuffs, a plain insignia-free shoulder patch on Dutch, pouch flaps and snaps, canteen and pack flaps with straps and buckles, legging hooks, boot soles (a welt band) with eyelets and crossed laces, boot straps and buckles (Selwyn), Trudi's apron pocket and bow, the Cousins' modest finishing (collar buttons, cardigan pockets, a waist seam, shoe buttons; nothing that changes how they read). Vertex-colour grime (.g AO) darkens hems, trouser bottoms and soles, and leather gets a fine scuff mottle; fabric weave and leather grain stay the kit shader's existing detail textures. Budget: people.json body_tris 8000 (was 6000; the head and hands are spared, so this keeps the trunk where it was), hair_tris 2600 (new cap), kit_tris 7500 (was 4500), and a hero_tris 19 600 ceiling: the kit is decimated to min(kit_tris, hero_tris - body - parts - hair), so a person with long hair gets a little less kit; the small finishing pieces (120 faces or fewer: buttons, buckles, laces, welts) are spared by the decimation and the big shells give way. The hero cap in ART_BIBLE section 5 and test_people_bodies HERO_TRIS goes 15 000 -> 20 000: the people average 18 800 triangles (were 12 400), the most is 19 450; the worst realistic scene (3 bots, Jakub, a companion, Selwyn) adds about 40 000 triangles over before, against about 240 000 for 24 zombies, with the automatic mesh LODs unchanged (PeopleBodies.mesh generate_lods). The rig, bones, weights scheme, hitboxes, shape keys, animation and gameplay are untouched (every new piece skins to the garment vertex it sits on); test_people_bodies and test_zombie_body pass. Not measured: rendered frame cost (headless has no GPU); check with --frame-log in the owner's next session.

T-135, 2026-10-29: one more companion per map, like Jakub (the owner: "ADD AN ADDTIONAL NPC ON EACH MAP LIKE JAKOB"). MapCompanion (gravelight/world/map_companion.gd, CompanionTalk; data/gravelight/companions.json): Map 0 Trudi Hanke (the barmaid, who runs out of the inn in round 1's opening: from round 2 she is hiding in the stable; her fate after the opening was unstated, now she can join you), Kaltenbrunn Fw. August "Gustl" Ammer (the quartermaster who fed the labourers, locked in the barracks stores; a German NCO, so no insignia anywhere, like Jakub's greatcoat), Totenhafen Hinnerk Petersen (new: the old harbour pilot who refused to take the cargo ship out at night and was shut in the signal tower; Sister Agnieszka, the lore's first pick, was rejected because Map 4 already says she went with the cargo), Fernflug Jette Maurer (the wind-tunnel technician who refused to fly the glider, in the station's yardmaster's hut in act I; she comes with you through acts II and III on the carried flag jette_with_you + her gun jette_gun, campaign.json carry_flags "jette_*", and stays behind at Groom Lake: the rocket seats four). Lighter than Jakub: no cage, breakout, tonics or wall-buys. It waits at its spot (snapped to the navmesh once it has synced; no map regenerated), calls out when a survivor is within 26 m but not 6 m, and a talk opens Jakub's choice card (JakubChoice, now generic: title, question, the unarmed line): GIVE A GUN takes the gun in your hands (never your only one, never a wonder weapon), NOT YET and it comes with its melee. With you it is a squad bot (BotSquad.add_brain with the companions.json brain options: no perks, no shopping, half-price ammo on the one it follows, accuracy 1.5), takes 60% damage (it has no perks; at 100% a round-1 Fernflug zombie downed it in two swings), can be revived, and a bleed-out leaves it out cold for the match (Jette is then left behind). Each round start it gives the one it follows a gift while they stand within 15 m: Trudi and Jette a grenade, Gustl a magazine for the gun in hand, Petersen 250 Scrip. Offline only, like Jakub. pids 10-13 (Jakub 9); MapRoot treats any companion's down as a companion's (its own bleed-out clock), VOManager speaks them as TRU/AMM/PET/JET (AMM/PET/JET on the companion channel; TRU keeps the story channel her opening lines use), Net.DISPLAY names them, RemoteBody has their fallback outfits and AI name tags. 42 new lines (COMP_CALL, MEET, JOIN_ARMED, JOIN_UNARMED, GIFT, FALLEN, Jette's ARRIVE) baked with Kokoro; three new Kokoro voices in voice_cast.json (AMM, PET, JET). Three new modelled people built with the T-134 detail (people.json + make_kits.py kit_ammer: a closed grey storeman's coat over a plain tunic, a pencil in the breast pocket, a keys pouch, a plain side cap; kit_petersen: a navy double-breasted pea coat with horn buttons, a knitted roll-neck, a plain peaked pilot's cap, sea boots; kit_jette: work coveralls with rolled sleeves, chest pockets, a webbing belt, a spanner in the thigh pocket, a kerchief). Tests: test_map_companions (one per built map, pids, voices, lines, bodies, names, Jette's carry), smoke_map_companions (each map: on the navmesh in its zone, not a match player while waiting, Trudi absent in round 1, talk, GIVE A GUN, follows you back to the start, kills, the gift; Trudi's bleed-out; Jette arrives in act II armed). Bot gaps found and left: a companion standing at a window behind the human gets no line of sight through the frame (the zombie reaches through, the ray hits the wall beside it), as for any bot.

T-136, 2026-10-31: the savings bank (the owner: "create a bank in the first and second map where users can deposit points, the points work like a savings account each round the amount in there goes up 1%. the points will work in between matches. convert one of the buildings on the outside to the bank, add a character in there that is a scared bank teller"). BankAccount (gravelight/world/bank_account.gd, data/gravelight/bank.json): the balance lives in the profile (ProfileSave "bank": balance, deposited, withdrawn, interest), so it is there in every later match on any map; deposits and withdrawals at a branch; BankLedger adds 1% at every round start of every match on every map (rounded down, at least 1 on any balance, capped at 1,000,000; a typed slip by the Scrip shows it). Moving Scrip in or out goes through Economy.bank_move, which GameState's scrip_earned and the power-up drop counter skip: a withdrawal is not an earning (else depositing and withdrawing would farm drops). Shut in Classic Rules (no meta progression there, like the tier IV perks) and online (the profile is local and Scrip is the host's; a later pass could sync it). Branches: Map 0's woodshed by the west road is now the village's Darlehenskasse (gen_map00 build_bank_shed: hollow plank walls on the old footprint, a 1.7 m doorway in the north wall between the posts (1.2 m did not pass the navmesh's 0.5 m agent radius: nothing could walk in), the window lit, a green signboard DARLEHENSKASSE and a plate "Spar- und Darlehenskasse Kreuzweg · Zweigstelle"; the old OUT_SOLIDS block is gone; the navmesh fingerprints in test_map00_art are the T-136 bake), in GROUNDS behind the front door (1250) or the gate (750); Kaltenbrunn's is the pay office in the administration block's ground floor (C_ADMIN, behind Door_Admin 1000), west bay, beside the Second Wind machine, no map rebuilt. BankBranch builds the furniture at load in the branch's frame (bank.json at / yaw / width): a walnut counter with a baize top and front panels (one collider), a brass grille with the teller's window and a money tray, a ledger, an ink pot, a bell, a stamp, a lamp over the window (a shadowed OmniLight), a safe behind him (a collider), Kaltenbrunn's sign BERGMANNSKASSE KALTENBRUNN · LOHNBÜRO over his head. The teller: Erwin Lassmann, the Darlehenskasse's junior clerk (new people.json row + kit_teller: white shirt with sleeve garters, a dark waistcoat with a watch chain, a narrow tie, black shoes), a PatronBody: he clutches the counter, cowers when a zombie is within 12 m and for 2.5 s after a shot within 9 m (flinching), greets you once per match (Map 0: the bank is open, please don't let them in; Kaltenbrunn: he carried the cash box up from the crossroads and your account is safe), says a line on each deposit / withdrawal / empty request / shut bank, and begs you to shoot the zombies near him; 19 lines baked with Kokoro (TEL: a young voice, fast and breathy, a light German accent). BankCounter ("Bank: ǂ N saved, +1% a round", hold 0.25 s) opens the BankCard: [interact] deposit 1000, [jump] deposit all, [reload] withdraw 1000, [melee] withdraw all, [crouch]/[pause] leave; you stand still while it is open (like Jakub's card) and it closes when you are hurt or downed, the match leaves play, or after 25 s without a move. A till sound (gen_audio bank_till: the counter bell, the drawer, coins). Bots never bank. Tests: test_bank (interest maths, deposit/withdraw/accrue, saved with the profile, no earning, Classic shut, branches, teller data), smoke_bank (Map 0: the navmesh reaches the counter through the doorway, the greeting, the prompt, the card, DEPOSIT 1000 / ALL, 1% at a round start, he cowers at a shot; the balance in the saved profile; Kaltenbrunn: the navmesh reaches the pay office's counter, his Kaltenbrunn greeting, WITHDRAW ALL, nothing counted as earned). Balance note: 1% compounding per round is generous in long games (about x1.64 over 50 rounds) and a big balance lets a later match start rich; that is the owner's design, capped at 1,000,000.

T-137, 2026-11-01: the Marke (UPDATE 09 section 1; the binding canon is contract C1 in docs/U09_CONTRACTS.md; the owner: "the zombies come from a chip, a mark, not only a sickness"). Canon (CANON 2.2, LORE 2.5, TIMELINE): from June 1942 Abteilung III cut Nachtglas offcuts into the Marken, chips under the skin of the right hand or the forehead, sold as a loyalty and identity tag ("die Marke, die dich hält"; pay, rations and Scrip tallied against it; the Ledger counts the marked); a marked person who dies gets up, and the marks run only while the carrier reaches them (the Ledger on Earth, RR-7 on the Moon), so the Fernflug key turning puts every Marke out the same second as the hum (MAP4 6.8 gained that sentence). The Erwachen is explained as both things at once, the raw substrate in the mine and every marked guard who died that night; away from the mine, everything that rises was marked, which keeps every existing date and the Laboratory Wing's unmarked risen (Tomasz). Dated beats added, none moved: 14 Jun 1942 the first Marken (implant order 14/42), Aug 1942 Brück the first volunteer and the recruiting poster, May 1943 the labour gangs' overseers offered it and refusing, Jakub in Gallery Nine ("I have a name. It is enough to carry."), 20 Dec 1944, May 1945. Respect rule written into CANON 8: never a camp tattoo or a number, never on a forced labourer; it was sold to people who had a choice. Who knows what: Deacon names it from memory ("the mark in the right hand, or in the forehead"), quietly; Peg's Directive LANTERN item 1 now also says "retrieve the tokens" (the one existing canon text changed, logged in STORY_CHANGES with the diary pages); Wren has seen it on the road dead ("same hand, every time"); Fasbender refused it in 1942 and explains the carrier once (MARKE_RELAY); the Operator calls a marked kill "a valued customer, retained"; nobody says the word for it (CANON 8 kept). VO: every line in lines.json and VO_SCRIPT.md was searched for sickness / virus / plague / infection / disease / contagion / "in the water" and nothing in the game ever explained the dead that way (only curses and FAS_M0_FREQ2_01's raw-substrate truth), so no line was rewritten; 25 new lines (MARKE_FIRST and MARKE_KILL x3 for DUT/PEG/DEA/WRN/JAK, OPR MARKE_OPR x3, FAS MARKE_RELAY x2; VO_SCRIPT 25) baked with Kokoro (20) and OneCore (5) and added to the voice scripts (make_scripts.py regenerated, 25 ids in script_index.json). Trigger: VoManager._on_marke_kill on GLBus.marke_kill: the first Marke kill of a match is a story beat (the killer's MARKE_FIRST, the Operator 6 s later, Fasbender 18 s after through a new _later_story delayed form for story speakers), later ones roll MARKE_KILL (45 s, 20%, echo 12 s) and MARKE_OPR (120 s, 35%). Intel: five documents, INT_MARKE_ORDER (Kaltenbrunn, random pool), INT_MARKE_POSTER (fixed, the taproom's south wall west of the clipping), INT_MARKE_MEDIC (fixed, the Totenhafen infirmary's middle cot: a navy surgeon removed a token from a dead rating and watched it keep pulsing), INT_MARKE_TERMS (fixed, the Moon's reception desk: the Provider's form, the one contract-shaped text in Gravelight, "Death is not a cancellation event"), INT_MARKE_JAKUB (fixed, LORE 13.5, the Maintenance Stairs' second landing north wall: his refusal in the prop alphabet, transcribed); intel.json is no longer wholly extractor-owned (UPDATE 07 added the Map 4 rows by hand, so a plain extract_intel.py run drops eight rows and flips the line endings): the five rows and the three dossier pages were spliced into the HEAD files instead, and the LORE / CHARACTERS sources carry the same text for the next person. Gameplay (C1, host-authoritative): enemies.json "marke" (damage_mult 1.5, scrip_bonus 25, hand_chance 0.6, hitbox_m 0.07, glow 0.25..1.0); ZombieBody.marke_site_for(net_id, hand_chance) is a pure hash of net_id so a co-op puppet shows the host's site with nothing sent (puppet_activate derives it the same way); two "marke" hitboxes per body (RightHand, the front of Head; only the site's is live), kept out of _hitboxes (that list is the limb contract the body and emerge tests walk) but in hitbox_rids() so a shot never lands twice on one body; the glint is one shared unshaded ShaderMaterial on the gl_hum / gl_round_k globals (no per-frame work per zombie); take_damage on &"marke" multiplies and continues as the host limb (arms / head) with last_hit_bone set, so armour, wounds, flinch, death clips and the headshot path all follow; the three hitscan traces (weapon_holder, bot_aim, trapped_pilot) use the host limb's weapon multiplier for a Marke box so a forehead mark is never worse than a headshot; a kill from a Marke hit emits GLBus.marke_kill after the normal reward and pays the bonus through Economy.award (Double Scrip and the earn multiplier apply; a "THE MARK" slip through bonus_awarded) with Zombie.marke_bonus_for adding PerkManager's marke_tip when section 3 lands; GameState.stats marke_kills; GLBus.marke_hit tints the next hitmarker violet (a 50 ms latch, pure marke_latched for the test). Classic Rules keeps the weak point and pays nothing. No Marke on the Foreman (no rig), the Screamer, the shield cells and the Backorder ("marke": false); a severed right arm takes the hand's mark with it. Tests: test_marke (12: the table, the deterministic site and its 60/40 split, the built body's hitbox and glint, x1.5, the bonus once and doubled, Classic, no Marke on bosses, the lost hand, the hitmarker latch); test_story_props and test_map04d_data updated for the new fixed props. Worktree notes for the lead: the worktree checkout had LFS pointers only (git lfs checkout fixed it) and an empty .godot cache that --quit-after 900 never filled (godot --import did); tools/run_smokes.sh's filter is a shell glob, so the brief's "a|b|c" filter matches nothing: the five smokes were run one by one with the same command.

T-140, 2026-11-01: the zombies mode is renamed CLAUDE ZOMBIES (UPDATE 09 section 6, contract C2). Player-facing only: Game.GAME_MODE_NAME ("CLAUDE ZOMBIES") and Game.GAME_MODE_NAME_TITLE ("Claude Zombies", for prose) are the one place; Game.GAME_TITLE stays "TERMS & CONDITIONS" as the umbrella and project.godot config/name is unchanged. Folders (gravelight/), class and autoload names (GLSettings, GLBus), Game.Mode.GRAVELIGHT, data file names, save keys and ids (the hub door id "gl") do not change. Routed through the constants: the dev console banner, the Credits title and "the ... team" line (a {MODE} placeholder in the const text, replaced at build), the dev launcher header, its "menu" button and map list labels (const arrays, so the literal "CLAUDE ZOMBIES" there), the Gravelight main menu's window title ("CLAUDE ZOMBIES — TERMS & CONDITIONS"; nothing else set a window title). JSON that cannot reference a constant says CLAUDE ZOMBIES directly: the hub door title, the credits roll head in cards_m4.json, the "(Claude Zombies)" suffix on two Settings rows; the recording guide, release form and Play.bat text too (voice_scripts/*.txt edited in place and tools/voice/make_scripts.py changed to match; RECORDING_GUIDE.pdf is not regenerated). Kept as they are: "Operation Gravelight" and the call sign Gravelight in story, intel, dossiers, objectives and VO (the squad's mission and radio name; no VO line says the game's title, checked in data/gravelight/vo/lines.json: every hit is Glasmacher, the squad or Jakub using the call sign), GRAVELIGHT ONE (the aircraft), code comments, the bot planner's LLM system prompts (not shown to players), docs history and DECISIONS paragraphs. Docs: GDD.md, GDD_GRAVELIGHT.md, UI_KIT.md headings now "CLAUDE ZOMBIES (code name GRAVELIGHT)", README line. Wordmark: gravelight/ui/wordmark/claude_zombies.svg is built by tools/gen_wordmark_svg.py from Stardos Stencil Bold (OFL) converted to outlines with fontTools (Godot's SVG renderer has no text or filters): CLAUDE small and letter-spaced over a large ZOMBIES, violet #c07dff gradient fill with a dark ink edge, amber cracks clipped to the letters, two thin rules with a five-stroke tally (the fifth crossing) between them; the SVG's glow group is only a vector stand-in because the rasteriser ignores stroke-opacity, so gravelight/tools_src/render_wordmark.gd drops it, draws the lettering and bakes a real soft violet glow from the alpha into claude_zombies.png (2048 x 940, transparent). The same script renders claude_zombies_icon.png (256 px, the Z with cracks and tally on a dark tile) and copies it to web/favicon.png; claude_zombies_icon.ico (16 to 256 px, Pillow) sits beside it for the Windows build. MenuKit.wordmark(height_px) returns the png as a TextureRect (aspect kept); the Gravelight main menu uses it (250 px) in the old title slot, and the hub's right-hand card swaps its title label for it (150 px) on the CLAUDE ZOMBIES door. Tests: test_game_mode_name (constants, hub and credits titles, no "(Gravelight)" labels, wordmark and favicon sizes, MenuKit.wordmark).

T-138, 2026-11-01: online play, the WebRTC transport and the Supabase lobbies (UPDATE 09 section 2a/2b; contract docs/U09_CONTRACTS.md C3; plain words docs/ONLINE.md). Transport: Net keeps ENet (host_game / join_game, direct IP, now the lobby screen's "LAN (direct IP)" page) and gains `enum Transport {ENET, WEBRTC}`, `Net.transport`, `host_online(lobby)` / `join_online(lobby, token)`: RtcTransport (gravelight/net/rtc_transport.gd) builds a WebRTCMultiplayerPeer (host = peer 1, `create_server`; each client `create_client(id)` with a random id 2..2^31-1 the host picks), one WebRTCPeerConnection per pair, ICE servers from online.json (the `stun` list, `turn` when its urls are non-empty), trickle ICE, four extra unreliable-ordered channels matching the rpcs' transfer channels 1..4; it ends as a MultiplayerPeer, so every existing rpc runs unchanged and nothing above Net knows the transport. Desktop WebRTC is the webrtc-native GDExtension 1.2.2-stable (MIT, libdatachannel 0.24.6; addons/webrtc, Windows + Linux x86_64 debug/release only, binaries in LFS; the web build uses the engine's own, the .gdextension excludes "web"); it loads headless on this PC (`WebRTCPeerConnection.initialize` OK); without it `RtcTransport.available()` is false, online says so and LAN still works. Signaling: abstract NetSignaling (`connect_lobby(code, is_host)`, `send(to, kind, payload)`, signal `message(from, kind, payload)`, `close()`; one JSON wire format {from, to, kind, payload}, kinds hello / offer / answer / ice / bye, ids "host" or a random 12-char id, "*" = everyone; decode drops foreign, own, unknown, malformed and >32 KB messages), SupabaseSignaling (Realtime broadcast on `realtime:lobby:<code>`, Phoenix protocol vsn 1.0.0, broadcast self=false, heartbeat 25 s; the channel stays open the whole session so a "bye" arrives), MockSignaling + MockSignalServer (a TCPServer/WebSocketPeer relay on 127.0.0.1 that also serves a MockLobbyBackend: two headless instances meet without the internet). The client resends hello every 1.5 s until the host answers (broadcasts can be lost). Lobbies: the autoload `Lobbies` (LobbyService, gravelight/net/lobby_service.gd, after Net) with the C3 API (create / list / join / quick_match / heartbeat / set_ready / set_character / leave / start; signals lobbies_changed, joined, left, failed, match_starting) plus rejoin(), set_fill_bots / set_map / set_public, start_browsing (5 s); backends SupabaseLobbyBackend (REST with the anon key in apikey + Bearer; one retry when a request got no HTTP answer, never for a create) or MockLobbyService (in-memory or through the relay; `online.json "backend": "mock"`, `--mock-online[=port]`, `--mock-online-serve=port`). Codes: 6 letters from A-Z without I/O (24^6). Display name asked once, kept in the profile (`display_name`), shown in the roster and the lobby row. Quick Match = the open public lobby (for the map, if given) with the fewest players that has room, else a new public one with bots on. Supabase (supabase/migrations/0001_lobbies.sql, applied to project fkoebguijrmlqdqrsgdy by the lead; no pg_cron there, so the trigger and the client sweep): table `lobbies` (code pk, map_id, is_public, host_name, players jsonb, fill_bots, status, max_players, created_at, heartbeat_at, host_token), RLS on, column grants leave host_token out of every select, anon may select public not-closed rows and insert; a BEFORE INSERT trigger forces status/timestamps, sweeps, and limits 5 inserts a minute per host_token (an insert log table, so deleted rows still count), 1 open lobby per host_token, 30 a minute per client IP (x-forwarded-for when present), 500 open lobbies; no update/delete policy: `lobby_update(code, host_token, patch)` (whitelisted keys, refreshes heartbeat_at), `lobby_close`, `lobby_get(code)` (private lobbies by exact code), `lobby_join(code, player)` (appends to an open lobby with room; lobby_not_found / lobby_closed / lobby_full), `lobby_sweep()` (heartbeat older than 120 s, closed rows); all SECURITY DEFINER, execute granted to anon. The live round-trip (gravelight/tools_src/online_live_check.gd, headless, anon key only) passed: insert 201, list 200, get 200, join 200, a full lobby's join 400 lobby_full, heartbeat 200, a wrong token 400, reading host_token 401, a direct update 401, a second open lobby on one token 400 one_open_lobby, close 200 true, get after close null; the game's own backend passed the same 7 calls. Online bots (the T-031 deferral is gone): `fill_bots` adds roster rows {bot, peer 0} for every empty slot up to the lobby's max_players at the start (the roster goes out before the start rpc), MapRoot makes them bots only on the host (a BotSquad for the online session) and puppets on clients; NetMatch sends bot rows in the player stream, their inventories (structural changes at once, ammo at most once a second) and their shots (WeaponHolder._report_fire -> bot_fired); a bot never holds up the ready check. Failures: a join that isn't welcomed within connect_timeout_s (15) fails with "Couldn't connect to that lobby (timed out after 15 s)." and the screen goes back; the host leaving sends a reliable `_rpc_bye` and a signaling "bye" and keeps the old peer polled for 0.6 s before closing (else the goodbye was lost and clients saw a dead link), clients get "The host left the match." and the lobby screen (Net.reopen_lobby; no host migration); a connection that dies without a goodbye says "Lost the connection to the host." and offers Rejoin. Drop and rejoin: each client sends a session token (32 hex) in its hello; the host maps token -> pid; a client that drops mid-match keeps its pid for rejoin_grace_s (60): with fill_bots its body becomes a bot (Player.become_bot, a brain without the bot spawn kit, its own kit kept), without it the body steps out (Player.set_away: hidden, no collision, out of the zombies' "players" group and the co-op count); after the grace a bot keeps the slot or the player is removed. A rejoin (Lobbies.rejoin: lobby_get, since a playing lobby refuses lobby_join, then join_online with the saved token) gets the same pid, survivor and body: the host hands the body back, sends `_rpc_rejoin` (the client loads the running map) and on its load `resync_peer` (begin, the body's transform, kit and health, every live zombie's spawn, open doors, boards, away bodies, everyone's inventory; the rest comes with the next state snapshot). The token lives in the profile (`online_session`), so a rejoin works after a crash too. Host-only systems online: Jakub (companion_jakub.json "online" true) and the map companions (MapCompanion.attach no longer returns online) are simulated on the host exactly as offline; every client builds the same body as a non-bot puppet fed by the player stream (NetMatch sends the "companions" group's rows; on_players finds them by pid), keeps state, weapon and wounds in step through Net.mirror `_net_state` (with the join / out-cold banners), plays their lines through `_net_say`, ignores relayed events itself (CompanionNet); a client's Talk reaches the host through request_interact and the host opens the choice card on that client's screen (`_net_card`, offer/tonic sent without Objects), whose answer comes back through a whitelisted call that now carries the sender's pid (NetMatch.on_quest_request passes it to methods named `net_by_*`: net_by_choose / net_by_choose_tonic); a companion handing a teammate's gun back and the map companions' round gifts go through forwarded inventory ops (give_net_entry, gift_grenade, gift_mag). The Bell Dome's working field always runs on the host: a client's dome is only a look (`visual_only`) and the host plants the checked working copy (net_by_bell_dome: within 12 m of the planter, clamped numbers); every other peer gets the look (`_net_bell_dome`); a host's or a host bot's dome shows on every client. The bank opens online: each player's balance stays in their own profile (interest from every peer's own BankLedger at each relayed round start), the counter opens on the player's own machine (net_local), a client's deposit moves its Scrip on the host through `Net._rpc_bank_req` / `_rpc_bank_state` (at most the Scrip the client has); a withdrawal is taken from the client's balance at once and the host adds the Scrip, capped at max_balance per request since the host can't see a client's balance (a modified client could mint Scrip: accepted for co-op among friends). Other authority guards in gravelight/world and weapons (exfil, campaign, inn opening and survivors, the trapped pilot, the Crucible, traps, endings, the drummer) already mirror their presentation; left: the companions' campaign carry-over and the "profile" Jakub stay offline-only (online, Jakub is the Map 0 cage one), dynamic world nodes spawned before a rejoin (a power-up on the ground) aren't resent, the Totenglocke's hit-stop stays offline-only by design. Screens: gravelight/ui/menu_lobby.gd rewritten on Lobbies with one `_build_*` method per page (name, home with Quick Match / Create / Browse / Join by code / Rejoin / LAN, create, browse, join, busy, room, lan) for the C7 restyle; the main menu reopens it after the host left / a drop. Tests: test_online.gd (codes, row validation, the mock table's state machine incl. rate limit / one open lobby / expiry, quick match, rejoin tokens, signaling encode/decode, ICE config, error keys, online.json holds only the anon key (its JWT role is "anon"), the screens build, the bank reply, companion card wiring, dome args), test_coop.gd +2 (bots in the roster); smokes smoke_webrtc (two headless instances over WebRTC + the mock relay: browse, join by code, a refused duplicate survivor, ready, map00, 2 rounds with 1 bot the client sees moving with its Thompson, drop -> bot -> rejoin as pid 2 / wren, host leaves -> "The host left the match.") and smoke_lobby (create, browse, join by code, full, ready/start with the row "playing" and joins refused, bad code, the 15 s timeout, 120 s expiry, quick match both ways, the rejoin window). Commits end with the session's own Co-Authored-By line (CLAUDE.md: "the line the session gives").

T-139, 2026-11-01: the web build, the graphics profiles and the minimum-spec performance pass (UPDATE 09 section 2c/2d, contract C4). Export presets (export_presets.cfg, committed): "Windows Desktop" (x86_64 release, the pck embedded, no console wrapper, the project icon, product name CLAUDE ZOMBIES (Game.GAME_MODE_NAME, §6); tests, docs, reference, voice folders, tools/, addons/gut excluded; gravelight/tools_src kept for the launcher's tool scenes and --frame-log) and "Web" (the Compatibility renderer: project.godot rendering_method.web="gl_compatibility", which is also what Godot forces on the web; thread support off, so the single-threaded web_nothreads template is used and no COOP/COEP headers are needed: we measured nothing that needs threads; canvas resize policy adaptive; custom shell web/shell.html: a dark loading screen with a violet progress bar and MB counter, no video; the page title CLAUDE ZOMBIES; tools_src and gravelight/tools_src also excluded). Textures: Godot 4.7.2 has no per-platform import override and no export-time size limit, and rewriting the .import files per build would reimport the whole library twice, so an editor export plugin (addons/gl_web_export, enabled in project.godot; editor-only, never in an export) caps every VRAM-compressed texture over 1024 px in exports whose features include "web": decompress, Lanczos to 1024 on the long side, mipmaps, recompressed as a PortableCompressedTexture2D in the same family (S3TC / BPTC / RGTC for normal maps); uncompressed 2D textures (UI, the minimap floors, fonts) are left alone; external textures handed over inside a scene's walk are skipped (returning them embedded a copy in every scene: map00 grew 9.5 -> 19.5 MB in the first try). 35 textures capped, 108 Mpx fewer; the single-pack web export went 462 -> 360 MB. Map packs: tools/build_web.sh runs tools_src/web_pack_lists.gd (each map scene's recursive dependency closure; a file goes into a map's pack only when that map, or only Fernflug acts, need it, nothing else in the project needs it, no script / JSON / config names it by path or uid, and it is not a script or JSON table; the map scenes themselves always go), plus the deferred "game" packs: match-only content the start-up and menu closures don't need (VO, the Poly Haven / ambientCG / Sketchfab / OpenGameArt libraries, our wonder weapons, world objects and arms; characters stay in the main pack because §7's 3D menu builds zombies and the squad from them), split into chunks of at most 110 MB of source (each exported pack under 70 MB: static hosts cap single files near 100 MB). tools/web_build.py writes a temporary export_presets.cfg with "Web Main" and one "Web Pack <name>" per pack in Godot's customized export mode ("remove" / "keep" per file, so a pack holds exactly its files and none of their dependencies), Godot exports them headless, the committed file is put back, and the engine files and packs get content-hashed names (cz-<hash>.wasm, cz-main-<hash>.pck, packs/<name>-<hash>.pck, index.html patched, mainPack set) so Vercel can cache them for a year without a stale build ever sticking; packs/manifest.json (no-cache) lists the packs per map. MapPacks (autoload): `await MapPacks.ensure(map_or_scene)` before every map change (the map select, Net._begin_match, Campaign.travel, the console's act / campaign commands); on the desktop and in a single-pack export it returns true at once without yielding (nothing changed for the tests and smokes); on the split web build it fetches the manifest, then each missing pack with HTTPRequest into the browser's in-memory /tmp (not IndexedDB: 230 MB there could hit quotas; the browser's HTTP cache does the re-visit) with a progress overlay, and mounts it with load_resource_pack; the game packs start downloading in the background as soon as the menu is up; Campaign.scene_exists knows the packed maps; Arms no longer remembers a missing GLB while packs are pending; `--packs-from=<dir> --packs-selftest` mounts packs from disk and instantiates every map (a desktop check of a web build). Sizes (the real build after rebasing on §1 + §6, MB): engine 37.7, main pack 90.9, html/js/icons 0.3: 129.0 to reach the menu (target < 150; ~88 over the wire with compression); game packs 56.8 + 47.4 + 63.2 + 68.9, maps: Crossroads 22.2, Kaltenbrunn 5.5, Totenhafen 4.2, Fernflug shared 2.7 + acts 0.3-0.5; 368-387 MB before the first match. Checks: the main pack alone boots with 0 errors; the self-test (run from build/web so nothing falls back to the project folder) mounts and instantiates all seven maps with 0 errors; tools/serve_web.sh serves it and curl returns 200 with application/wasm, text/javascript, application/octet-stream and application/json. Windows: tools/build_windows.sh -> build/windows/ClaudeZombies_0.0.1-m0/ClaudeZombies.exe (571 MB, the pck embedded, T-140's claude_zombies_icon.ico, product name CLAUDE ZOMBIES from §6) and build/ClaudeZombies_0.0.1-m0.zip (383 MB); the exe runs --headless --quit-after 60 with exit 0 and no error. The web page links T-140's web/favicon.png (copied into build/web). Vercel: web/vercel.json (application/wasm, immutable year-long caching for the hashed .wasm / .pck / .js, no-cache for the page and the manifest, no COOP/COEP), web/middleware.js (Routing Middleware, Edge, no packages, 39 lines: without CZ_PASSWORD the site is open; otherwise a small password form POSTs to /gate, a correct answer sets cz_gate = sha256("cz:" + password), HttpOnly, 30 days; checked under Node: open, form, 401 wrong, 303 + cookie right, pass-through with it, 401 forged), tools/deploy_web.sh (build, then `npx vercel deploy --prod --archive=tgz --cwd build/web`; needs `npx vercel login` once: NEEDS_DEVIN), tools/serve_web.sh. Graphics: GLSettings.is_web(), is_compat() (the renderer, or compat_override for tests), the "web" preset (no AA at all: no MSAA by the work order and the Compatibility renderer has no FXAA / TAA, which it warned about in the first census; 85% bilinear, low shadows, no AO / SDFGI / SSIL / SSR / volumetric fog, coarse LODs), offered as "Browser" in Settings > Video only on Compatibility; detect_preset(adapter, type, method) (Compatibility -> web; CPU / virtual / llvmpipe / integrated / Iris / UHD / "Radeon Graphics" / 680M / 780M -> low; a discrete NVIDIA / AMD -> high, also by name when the type is unknown; anything else, Intel Arc, Apple -> medium; never ultra) runs once on first launch (no saved graphics_preset; never headless, so tools and smokes keep High). WebProfile.apply (MapRoot after ShadowProxies, again deferred; the menu backdrop): SDFGI / SSIL / SSR / SSAO / volumetric fog off; a map that wanted volumetric fog keeps its own fog thickened x1.3, or gets depth fog in the volumetric fog's lit colour (end 2.5 / density, clamped 30-300 m); FogVolumes hidden and the code-built ones (landscape field fog, the west fog) not built on Compatibility (their fog shader does not compile there: the census showed "shader type fog not supported"); sun shadows to 40 m; MSAA off; at most 4 shadowed omni / spot lights per zone (ZoneVolume; lights outside every zone count as one zone; the ones nearest the spawn keep it; lights carried by actors left alone); Zombie.anim_lod_scale 0.5 (anim.json lod distances halved); GPUParticles3D amount_ratio halved; Snowfall.density_scale and Effects.particles_k halved; idempotent. No collider, hitbox, navmesh, timer or prompt changes. Performance (docs/PERFORMANCE.md, new; linked from docs/gravelight/PERFORMANCE.md): the minimum spec is a laptop with Iris Xe or Radeon 680M at 1080p; the targets 60 FPS on Low on the desktop and 30+ in the browser with 24 zombies, 3 bots and the companions on every map. Measured here: the rendered census through WSL + Xvfb (tools/wsl_perf.sh, perf_marks --map / --preset / --quick / --no-webprofile; software GL, so counts are real and FPS is not) and the headless CPU census of every map (tools/perf_census.sh, --probe). Browser profile before / after (all passes, shadow renders included): Crossroads Inn 12,068 draw calls / 9,533 objects / 7.14M primitives -> 5,804 / 4,024 / 1.74M (the first profile, with cube shadows and 4 sun cascades, only reached 9,524 / 7,450 / 6.64M, so the browser's kept omni shadows went dual-paraboloid (2 renders, not 6; T-070 kept cubes on the desktop for looks, the browser profile is the simpler look by design) and the sun 2 cascades); Totenhafen 6,253 / 4,021 / 1.02M -> 3,303 / 3,009 / 639k (17 -> 9 shadowed lights); Kaltenbrunn 1,448 draw calls, the Fernflug acts 478-868. The inn and Totenhafen are still over C4's 2,500 / 200k browser budget (their landscape and lights: next levers listed in PERFORMANCE.md). CPU (headless): scripts ~9-16 ms a frame on every map, zombies 0.15-0.25 ms each per tick and the bot brains 1-1.6 ms each lead; not changed (gameplay AI), listed as the next lever. Fixes: the T-136 bank branch's ~65 boxes and cylinders (each a draw call and a caster in the lamp's cube shadow) merge into one mesh per material at load (Kaltenbrunn's branch 67 -> 14 shadow casters, the map's shadow-casting instances 709 -> 659; same meshes, materials and places); PatronBody (the T-134 people) poses its limbs with the zombies' anim.json LOD (every frame within 15 m of the camera, 2nd to 35 m, 4th beyond; the state runs every frame), no measurable change on Map 0 where the patrons stand near the camera; Compatibility guards (no screen-space AA / TAA there, the SSAO / SSIL / SDFGI / volumetric quality calls skipped, the code-built FogVolumes not made). Totenhafen's 11 shadowed lights outside every zone are window moon shafts across the map plus the five sea-mouth spotlights (5 in one place, inside the per-zone budget of 6). Not measurable here: real FPS (the owner's --frame-log on the RTX, and a browser on an Iris Xe laptop: NEEDS_DEVIN); the Forward+ Low census through lavapipe is in PERFORMANCE.md where it finished. Tests: test_web_profile (the preset table, detection on 17 adapter strings, WebProfile on a synthetic scene and its idempotence, the fog cases, the shadow cap's order, the texture cap, MapPacks on the desktop).
T-142, 2026-11-01: relief squads (UPDATE 09 section 4, the "living war" half; contract C5; the owner: "relief squads of 3-6 soldiers from the map edge... they shout, mostly get overrun, their dead rise as zombies (the mark); sometimes one survives to round end; lootable supplies"). Data `data/gravelight/squads.json` (a new GLData table "squads"): global from_round 4, chance_per_round 0.35, max_active 1, cooldown_rounds 3, size 3-6, stay_seconds 60-150, survivor_chance 0.25, scrip_share 0.25, overrun_bias 0.8 (each leaver's chance to be caught, so about 0.75 x 0.8^n of squads are lost outright and most of the rest lose all but a few), plus skip_special_rounds, rise_delay_s 4-8, hold_m 6-12, retreat_hp_frac 0.3, one revive each, down 12 s, damage_mult 0.55 and the loot table; seven kits and the factions per map: map00 Crossroads Inn Home Army partisans (0.6) and a Soviet scout patrol (0.4); map01 Kaltenbrunn Home Army (0.55) and Volkssturm deserters, old men only (0.45); map02 Totenhafen British commandos from the sea (0.6) and a Kriegsmarine shore party deserting the pens (0.4); map04a Thalbach an off-target US paratrooper stick (0.6) and Volkssturm (0.4); map04c Groom Lake MPs / range guards; map04b (a plane in flight) and map04d (the Moon) none, said in their _doc. Edge spawns: 3-5 per faction, each in the always-open start zone's far side (>= 20 m from the start) or at the outer edge of a later zone (used only once a path through the open zones exists: ReliefSquad.reachable with the director's nav mask), Map 0 on its field-edge spawn markers; every point verified headless with the new probe_squad_spawns tool (snap <= 1.0 m, >= 20 m from the start, a path to it; four first picks moved: an island in Totenhafen's pen hall and Thalbach's canteen corner had no path, one Kaltenbrunn point sat on the mesh border). Canon call (CANON 2.2 says the Marke was German-side and "far from the mine, everything that rises was marked"): factions carry "marked"; the Volkssturm and the Kriegsmarine are marked (German volunteers, the Totenhafen detachment) and rise with a Marke weak point; the Allied factions are unmarked and rise because every squad map sits near raw substrate (2.2: unmarked people who die near raw substrate can still rise: the Kaltenbrunn seam, Totenhafen's lead coffins, Thalbach's impellers, the Nevada stone), so their risen carry no Marke; the barks never name it. Volkssturm boys were left out on purpose so no child rises as a zombie; the old men are played with dignity (no armband, no insignia). Bodies without Blender work: ZombieBody.build_dressed (the living human_base GLB in an existing zombie kit's clothes, soldier / villager / sailor, recoloured by a per-faction palette; risen: the zombie base in the same clothes) and two new primitive gear pieces (beret, side_cap) beside the helmet, cap and sailor cap, coloured per faction; the Home Army's white-and-red armband and the MPs' plain black brassard on the armband piece with its emblem surface recoloured (never the Wiederkehr mark); SoldierBody is RemoteBody's puppet on a PersonRig.setup_mesh body (RemoteBody gained a _make_person_rig virtual). Actors: Soldier (CharacterBody3D on the player layer: zombies bump it, players' bullets pass through), SoldierBrain (the utility brain cut down: leave > retreat under 30% > revive a mate once > fight > follow; a fan 6-12 m from the player nearest the leader on the leader's side; BotPerception's threat score plus a far term; skills.json "recruit" aim with reaction, settling error and fire tolerance, bursts, magazines; no economy, eggs or commands), SoldierKits (looks pure over kit + seed so puppets and risers match), ReliefSquad (the roll, spawn, hold, pull-out, overrun, survivor, rises, loot, barks, a perf meter). Zombies hunt soldiers through their existing prey hook (Zombie._update_prey: is_catchable / struck), handed out every 0.5 s (1 zombie per soldier, 3 while leaving; doomed leavers take x3). The Marke at work: ZombieDirector.rise_at(position, kit) activates a pooled zombie outside the round queue (take_extra_spawn: it must die before the round ends), dresses it as the soldier (SoldierKits.dress_risen; the tag rides the zombie's "person" meta so co-op clients dress the puppet), clears the Marke for unmarked factions (Zombie.clear_marke) and plays the get-up; rises wait for a running round; the first rise of a match draws SQD_RISE from Deacon / Wren / Dutch / Peg (2 lines each). Budget rule (exact): ZombieDirector.actor_budget() = max(0, rounds.json max_active - soldiers alive), soldiers alive = standing or down; RoundManager.can_spawn checks alive < max_active - actor_reserve (set by ZombieDirector.set_soldiers_alive); the round's queue is never cut, only spread out while soldiers stand, so a round has the same kills as without them plus the risen soldiers, and the active actors never pass 24. Scrip: a soldier's kill pays the nearest living player floor(0.25 x Economy.kill_value) (at least 1; the zombie's own "scrip" row when it has one) through Economy.award, so Double Scrip and Ledger Lager apply; the soldier shoots with by_player 0 (no hit Scrip, no Marke bonus of its own). Event flags: GameState.EVENT_FLAGS boss_fight / holdout / cutscene, GameState.in_event() and hold_flag_while(flag, node) (the flag stays on while any registered node lives); boss_fight set when a HomageBoss enters (all homage bosses: the Cousins, the Kranführer, the Bordwart, the Test Pilot, the Account Manager) and when the Cousins Round begins, re-synced on _boss_die / _vanish / the round over (the Foreman and Screamer are round specials, not boss fights); holdout set in QuestManager._start_holdout, cleared when the last ends or on clear(); cutscene held by the exfil cutscene (the Fernflug act transitions are exfil cutscenes), quest cutscene runners (the requiem), the London window and both endings. A flag mid-squad despawns it next frame (no rises, no loot); special rounds are skipped too. Loot: the squad's first death leaves a SquadCrate (an AmmoBox at price 0, 4 refills), a dead soldier's gun as a WeaponParcel anyone may take (for_pid 0: WeaponParcel opened for that), a SquadPouch of 2 grenades (40%); all gone after 90 s; the survivor leaves a crate. Online: host-authoritative; NetMatch sends soldiers like zombies (a reliable spawn: map, faction, seed, weapon, index, position; "gone"; a 10 Hz 17-byte snapshot: id, position, yaw, anim flags, state; Net._rpc_ssnap on the zombie snapshot channel 4, since RtcTransport declares only channels 1-4, and _rpc_sev) and clients build Soldier puppets; the HUD line goes as an "arrive" event. Barks: 98 lines SQD_<FACTION>_<CAT>_NN (ARRIVE and CONTACT 3, HURT / DOWN / LEAVE / SURVIVE 2 per faction; period English with Polish / Russian / German words; no slurs) and 8 SQD_RISE, written in VO_SCRIPT 26, extracted, baked with Kokoro (gen_vo.py gained cast_key: voice_cast entries named after an id prefix, SQD_AK ... SQD_MP, give speaker SQD one voice per faction; there is no Russian accent profile, so the Soviet scouts use polish_light as a placeholder), category emotions and cooldowns in voice_cast / rules, SQD in data/voice/cast.json ([ACTOR]) and the voice scripts regenerated (slot_sqd 98 lines, 106 ids in script_index.json). The JSON files were spliced to keep the repo's formatting (extract_vo writes indent 1; the repo has indent 2; voice_cast keeps its compact rows). Launcher: "Relief squad test (Map 0, round 6, a squad forced on the next round)". Docs: BOT_AI 12, ENEMIES 3, VO_SCRIPT 26. Tests: test_relief_squad (15), test_soldier_brain (9), smoke_squads, probe_squad_spawns. Performance (smoke_squads, Map 0, 6 soldiers + about 22-24 zombies + 3 bots, 300 frames at 60 Hz headless): the soldiers' brains 0.439 ms a frame on average (max 14.3 ms on a frame where a soldier's shot killed a zombie: the death's gore and ragdoll run inside the shot, the same cost as a player's kill), the three bots' brains 1.863 ms, 2.302 ms together, under the 2.5 ms budget; the smoke fails if the soldiers' share reaches 2.5 ms. smoke_squads: 6 Home Army soldiers on the navmesh, budget 24 -> 18, 415 shots, 14 kills, 246 Scrip shared, all six overrun and risen in their own clothes without a Marke, 13 loot drops, round 4 still ended after the rises, and a second (Soviet) squad despawned cleanly when boss_fight was set. Tuning note: at damage_mult 0.55 a squad kills about one zombie per five seconds at round 4; it is a knob in squads.json if they feel useless.

T-143, 2026-11-01: more people on Kaltenbrunn and the Crossroads Inn, and NPCs and bots that walk into walls (UPDATE 09 section 4; the work order calls the Inn "Map 1" and Kaltenbrunn "Map 2", the code ids are map00 and map01). People: ExtraPeople / ExtraPerson / PersonCard (gravelight/world/extra_people.gd, extra_person.gd, gravelight/ui/person_card.gd; data/gravelight/people_extra.json) rather than more MapCompanions, because a MapCompanion JOINS the squad (a Player body, a brain, a pid) and these stay where they are: a PatronBody (no collider, so nothing to push or pathfind; PatronBody.setup takes a "person" key so a body can wear a people.json kit under another id), a Talk interactable (hold 0.35 s) that opens a card (name, bio, what they say now, up to three options on interact / reload / melee, crouch or pause to leave; closes when you are hurt or down), at most one favour, round-start and down reactions, a minimap note marker (MapState.add_marker) and, offline only, like the companions. No new body pipeline: the kits are the existing ones (miners rother / sobotta / petersen, medic wiesner, switchboard operator brandt, deserter kubiak, widow pohl, courier kroll). Kaltenbrunn: Alois Brzezina and two miners in the Salt Chapel's west aisle (3 bodies, two cowering, one speaks; favour: fetch a safety lamp from the barracks bunks, 500 Scrip and a chalked "short way" marker on the map; if Jakub is with you in the room he and Alois greet each other, MIN_PEOPLE_JAKUB_01 / JAK_PEOPLE_MINERS_01), Matthias Roth the medic in the barracks (a German orderly with no insignia who treated the labour gangs too; favour: the infirmary bandages from the bunk room's north wall; then for 2 rounds he is a revive point: a player down within 9 m is got up after 2.5 s, via CoopState.revive and Player.revive, no Scrip to anyone), Marta Seidl the switchboard operator at the Administration Block's switchboard (favour: a radio valve from the radio room upstairs; reward one free intel ping, MapState.reveal_zombies 70 m for 10 s; she relays the Operator: when an OPR "SLIP" line starts she says "Someone is counting on this line."), and the quartermaster is Ammer's own card (T-135 already made him the quartermaster, so no new body: MapCompanion gained a Talk card, PersonCard, when companions.json gives him a "trade": TAKE HIM WITH YOU / GIVE YOUR GUN as before, and TRADE 1500 Scrip for a random ammo refill or a grenade pack (+2), once a round, with COMP_TRADE / BROKE / ONCE lines). Crossroads Inn: Gerd Lanz, a nineteen-year-old deserter in the hayloft (no favour item: hand him the gun in your hands, he keeps it, and he covers the stable door for the rest of the round with that gun's damage, 75% hits, a shot every 1.4 s within 18 m in sight, no Scrip for his kills), Hanne Ebert, a carter's widow in upstairs Room 3 (fetch her husband's lamp from the cart in the yard; Door_Stable, the kitchen back door, costs 0 for 2 rounds, a 300 Scrip purse instead if it is already open), and Lotte Hager, a courier outside the Gate (no favour: 200 Scrip once a round for a tip, the Requisition Crate marked on your map for 90 s; CrateManager has no peek at the next result, so "a free Crate spin hint" is where the Crate is, not what it holds). Timed rewards count rounds in ExtraPeople.rounds_after_grant / rounds_after_start (granted mid-round the rest of that round is the first). 55 new lines baked with Kokoro (category PEOPLE_<KIND>; 6 new cast voices MIN MED RAD DES WID COU in voice_cast.json; the same run baked ten JAK_M4* lines that were in lines.json without audio). Walls: gravelight/tools_src/probe_npc_walls.gd (NpcWallProbe, scene probe_npc_walls.tscn; --map --minutes --speed --bots --no-companions, --spots / --scan to ask the navmesh, --debug-paths) runs a map with the human on player bot mode, 3 bots and every companion joined beside the human, samples every walker every 0.25 s and flags one that wants to move (intent over 0.3 for 80% of 3 s), went under 5 cm and has a wall within 40 cm ahead (a sphere cast), and statically checks every person's spot against the navmesh and a 0.3 m body, the survivors' walks from hiding place to post, and a bot's stand point for each wall-buy and ammo box (notes only: a wall-buy sits in its wall, a NavBlock is the fence round a box). Root causes found: (1) the Inn's upstairs survivors walk the raw navmesh path with no collider: Brandt and Kroll's walk from Room 2 ran along the doorway's jamb (nav points 0.25 m from it; a 1.5 m door) and every inner point floated 0.5 m over the floor (the navmesh sits 0.25 to 0.75 above it); InnSurvivor.shape_path now densifies the path to 0.3 m, pushes every point 0.5 m off the walls (eight chest-high rays) and puts it on the floor (a ray down); (2) a standing bot was shoved into a wall by a neighbour's personal-space push (Dutch against the stairwell wall in the Inn's taproom, 3.9 s, his goal upstairs behind the shut door): BotLocomotion.tick drops the push when a wall is within 0.55 m along it and the bot has no goal to walk to (wall_ahead); (3) BotLocomotion's stuck ladder counted a goal that came closer (a leader's slot walking toward a bot jammed on the Maintenance Stairs, Wren, 3.3 s) as progress and reset itself, so a pressed bot never reached its repath; progress through a nearer goal now also needs the body to have moved progress_move_m (0.2) since the last progress, and a second detector (locomotion.json stuck wall_repath_s 1.5 / wall_nudge_s 2.4: wants to move, under 0.3 m/s) re-plans and then steps off to a nearby navmesh point, independent of the ladder (Dutch pressed 5.0 s on the Mess crate's collider with the crate at that spot, Peg 4.3 s at the gatehouse's south-east corner on the headframe loop, whose two south points moved 0.7 to 1 m clear of the corner in routes_map01.json); (4) Petersen's signal-tower spot was 1.1 m off the navmesh (companions.json, moved; MapCompanion snapped it at runtime anyway). Before / after (probe, x4, 3 bots, companions joined; "pressed" = the bar above): map00 4 min 0 pressed, 3 survivor walks clipping a wall or floating, and (smoke run) Dutch pressed 3.9 s in the taproom -> 0 pressed, 0 spots (smoke_npc_walls, 4 min); map01 4 min 0 pressed, 10 min 2 pressed (Dutch 5.0 s, Peg 4.3 s; an earlier 10 min run 1: Wren 3.3 s) -> 0 pressed, 0 spots at 4 min (smoke_npc_walls); the 10 min after-run was not finished (the machine was shared with other workers and ran at about a tenth of speed), so the three 10 min cases are fixed by reasoning and the 4 min probe only; map02 4 min 0 pressed, 1 spot off the navmesh -> 0; map04a to d 0 pressed (a, b: the ammo boxes' NavBlock fences were first read as walls, now excluded). Not found: any NPC "near the Kar98k wall-buy" pressed against a wall on either map (the Kaltenbrunn gatehouse corner beside it is Peg's, above; the Inn's Kar98k is on the taproom's west wall and nothing walks there); left for the owner to say which one was meant. smoke_npc_walls (map00 and map01, 4 game minutes at x4 each, 3 bots, Jakub / Trudi / Ammer joined) fails on any body pressed over 3 s or any spot / survivor walk off; smoke_people_extra walks every favour, reward and the trade on both maps. Tests: test_people_extra (table, kits, voices, every named line and its audio, spec counts per map, the favour and reward state machines, the trade rules).

T-141, 2026-11-01: Ledger Lager, the points perk, and the bank on every map (UPDATE 09 section 3, contract C6; lore and numbers from docs/U09_LEDGER_LAGER.md). New tonic `ledger_lager` (perks.json; plan DIVIDEND DMS-VP-DIV-09; JINGLES.md section 16: the foxtrot, the fine-print panel, the human Rudolf "Rudi" Fenske; shape `register`, a code-built brass cash register 1.0 x 2.2 x 0.75 with a key bank, a drawer for the dispense tray and a tally drum that turns a digit for every Scrip its holder earns, gold at tier IV; cap glyph: the tally mark): 3000 / II Compound Interest 4000 / III Insider Rate 7500 / IV Golden Parachute 14000, prices unchanged by the soak. Effects: `Economy.award` and `Economy.grant` multiply positive amounts by `earn_mult(pid)` (PerkManager `scrip_earn_mult` 1.2, nearest whole Scrip, after Double Scrip) and now return what they paid (ScripBonuses' slips show the boosted number; the HUD "+N" is the scrip_changed delta, so it is boosted for free); `Economy.grant_raw` is the unboosted add and takes the crate's drummer refund (the only refund path), the dev console, Muster's starting kit and the tonic bottle's cap-full money; a negative grant (the Account Manager's cut) is never scaled. `Economy.price_for(pid, cost)` (discount rounded down: the house keeps the odd Scrip) is applied by `Economy.spend` and shown by `Interactable.price_str` ("[855, was 950]") in the door, wall-buy, crate, Crucible, trap, ammo box, pylon, perk and tier, Selwyn and survivor prompts. `BankAccount.rate_for(pid)` adds `bank_interest_bonus` (BankLedger passes the local player). Tier IV, permanent through the T-129 rules: ScripBonuses moves `golden_parachute_pct` of the wallet (cap 2500, `Economy.bank_move`, so no stats or power-up drops) at every round end for a player not downed in it, and `BankAccount.pay_severance` pays `severance_pct` of the savings (cap 2500) out of the account on a down; nothing in the game takes Scrip on a down, which is what makes the clause worth the price. `PerkManager.marke_tip(pid)` reads `marke_tip`; Zombie.marke_bonus_for (section 1, not in this branch) must add it to the Marke bonus. Classic Rules: tier I only, the bank shut. The bank on every map: bank.json `tellers` table plus branches: Totenhafen the Marinekasse on the crane office's south wall (Zahlmeistersmaat Karl Jessen, JES; the crane office's west wall keeps the manifest INT_M2_03, and the south wall's east end stays clear because it is the foot of the east stair: a first layout there broke smoke_map02's east-catwalk paths), Werk Thalbach the Lohnkasse in the yardmaster's hut (Ilse Ruhland, RUH), the Kranich a tiny Bordkasse at the centre body's forward bulkhead (Ruhland again: she stowed away with the cash box), Groom Lake the Range Finance Office at the south grille of Dee's cage (Pvt. Eugene Pruitt, PRU), Mondlicht Darkmatter's Accounts on the concourse's west wall (`construct`: a brass speaking-tube window, the Operator's OPR voice, no person). BankBranch builds a construct window or a teller (3 new people.json rows, GLBs built with tools/build_people.sh), says lines by the branch's own teller (8-10 lines each, categories BANK_GREET_M2/M4A/M4B/M4C/M4D plus the shared BANK_*, baked with Kokoro or OneCore through gen_vo), and each branch row carries a `nav_block` the generators (gen_map02, gen_map04_lib) cut out of the navmesh. Minimap: a `bank` POI per map read from bank.json by MapScan.scan_gl (the scene has no node), an icon and legend entry, and the new tools_src/refresh_mapdata_pois.tscn rewrites the POIs and signature of mapdata.json without the GPU bake (it refuses a map whose extent changed). Ledger Lager machines: map00 the cellar's south-east corner (34.1, -3.2, 30.2) yaw 90, map01 the pay office (-15.2, 0, 11) yaw -90, map02 the crane office's west wall (43.6, 0, 56.1) yaw 90, map04a the yardmaster's hut (3.6, 0, 57.45), map04b the forward bulkhead (-5, 0, 53.45), map04c the hangar's north wall (54, 0, 99.45), map04d the concourse's east wall (11.45, 0, 36.6) yaw -90; the branches (Marinekasse counter at (44.9, 58.3), the rest) are in bank.json. Scenes regenerated with the generators (navmesh re-baked; test_map00_art's two fingerprints re-based). Also: PERK_LEDGER first-drink lines (DUT PEG DEA WRN JAK), OPR_PERK_TIER4_LEDGER_01 (own category PERK_TIER4_LEDGER, played by PerkManager.set_tier, so other tonics' tier IV never draw it), BONUS_LEDGER lines and the bots' `earn` trigger, drawer and tally sounds and the jingle (gen_audio.py), PERK_LEDGER token, the Archive cabinet row, INT_M1_FENSKE_LEDGER (the second ledger, a logbook on the pay office floor, in LORE.md 13.2a, intel.json by hand because extract_intel.py no longer reproduces the committed file), PERKS_LORE.md entries per map (machine_lore.json via build_perk_lore.py). Bots: `ledger_lager` third in the perk plan and the upgrade plan (after the second tonic), in the agent's perk order, in the bonus-perk roll (trigger `earn`). Soak (botmode, Map 0, 3 bots, speed 4, seed 21, rounds 1-14; docs/gravelight/ECONOMY.md section 8): a survivor earns about 1700 a round at rounds 3-5, 2400-3400 at 7-9 and 4000-7000 from 10; +20% is 340 a round at round 3-5 and 480-680 at 7-9, so 3000 bought at round 3 is paid back in round 9-10 for all four survivors (net +3300..+5300 by round 14) and bought at round 5-6, when a bot first has the money, in round 11-12; the bots in fact bought it at rounds 10-12 (+5.0% squad earnings when only Peg held it from round 1). Neither before round 8 nor past round 15, so no price changed. Not done / for the lead: Zombie.marke_bonus_for reads marke_tip only once section 1 merges; smoke_map04c fails its burn-console steps on master too (the Kuendigung bots finish s12b_burn before the smoke gets there); extract_intel.py would drop 7 committed intel rows if re-run.

T-144, 2026-11-01: a much better main menu (UPDATE 09 section 7, contract C7; the lead's art direction). The scene: MenuScene (gravelight/ui/menu_scene.gd; MenuBackdrop is now a thin subclass, the Minehead Yard box set is gone), built in code from real assets, never a video. The set is gravelight/ui/menu_inn.tscn, cut once out of Map 0 by gravelight/tools_src/gen_menu_scene.gd: instance map00.tscn, keep Dressing minus the interiors (Taproom, Upstairs, Stable, Cellar, OldCellar, Staircase, Hearth, Bar, Polish, the inner door frames), StaticBatches minus every batch wholly inside the inn's or the stable's inner volume or below ground, the outdoor PropScatter items, Landscape, Snowfall, the Moon and the environment; strip physics, navigation, every light but the Moon, audio and every script but the scenery ones (snowfall, landscape, prop_scatter, baked_multimesh, nature_rows, draft_curtain); then merge the batches per material and shadow mode (gen_map00's 12 m cells only help the game's culling): 81 meshes, 5.4 MB of text; re-run it after gen_map00. Shot A stands on the west road at (-17, 1.5, -10.5), 20-25 m from the inn's north-west corner, looking across at the gable with the Moon above it (MenuScene aims the Moon light so the sky dome draws it there; the camera moved 3 m south of its first spot because a pole of the field telephone line stood 2.5 m in front of the lens). Our own set in front of the inn: a split-rail fence (Props.fence) 9.6 m out, the walkers' lane just outside it angled toward the camera's right, an end post and a dead tree they fade behind, a rust drum with flames, embers and smoke, two crates, drifts; warm amber panes in the inn's five north and west windows (in the wall plane so they read at this angle; a silhouette crosses one every 26 s), three unshadowed spill lights under them, boards on the windows the tearers work. Map 0's distant war is its own MapAmbience under the set with the menu's timings patched onto map00_ambience.json at load: artillery every 8-20 s at 4-8 km on bearings in shot, the boom delayed by distance as in the game; bombers every 80-100 s, 3-5 planes at 110-140 m so they cross the frame; fighters, cook-offs, the dog and the plane-down off; the searchlights and flares as the map has them. Zombies: real ZombieBody kits on ZombieRig skeletons (6 walkers: soldier, villager, miner in mixed builds; 2 tearers), each driven by an AnimationPlayer on a menu copy of the ZombieClips clips with the root-motion track removed (MenuScene moves the walkers down the lane at 0.72-0.9 m/s and scales the 1.3 m/s shamble clips to match; the tear clips loop), the Marke glint on the hand or forehead from the game's net_id hash, scaled 2.6x (the game's 1.5 cm bead is invisible at 10 m), gl_hum pulsed by the scene; at the end of the lane a walker dissolves (the skin shader's inst_dissolve) behind the post and comes back off the left of the frame. No AI, no director, no spawns. The squad: PersonRig bodies (the T-134 kits) posed every frame through PersonRig.drive, the same solver RemoteBody and PatronBody use (people have no idle clips): Dutch standing with a cigarette (a drag every 7 s, the ember brightens, a thin CPUParticles wisp from the hand bone), Peg standing, wrist up to her watch every 12 s with her head down to it, Wren on a crate head down over a notebook with the pencil moving, Deacon on a crate with a Thompson (WeaponModels) across his knees and a rag working along it; everyone breathes. Camera: shot A dollies 2 m right over 40 s with a 0.3 deg sway, then a 1 s cross-fade (a snapshot of the last frame fading out over the new angle; the web variant dips through the night colour since a read-back is slow in a browser) to shot B over the squad's shoulders toward the fence for 30 s, and back; the FOV breathes 0.7 deg. Hooks: ProfileSave.fernflug_beaten() (the new profile flag fernflug_beaten, set when the "kuendigung" ending is noted; older profiles read endings_seen) shows a violet point of light with a halo on the Moon; ProfileSave.egg_done("map00_radio") plays the gramophone record and faint radio static at the upstairs window (every completed quest is now recorded in the profile's eggs_done; egg_done also reads the progression main-egg rewards and counts Fernflug's egg as its ending). Budgets: desktop at most 4 shadowed lights (the Moon, 2 splits at 45 m, and the barrel; everything else unshadowed, enforced after the build), no SDFGI, SSIL or SSR, volumetric fog plus a low FogVolume over the ground; the web / Compatibility variant (GLSettings.is_web / is_compat, T-139) keeps 3 walkers and 1 tearer (half), one shadowed light, depth fog, half the snow and flames, and on the real Compatibility renderer WebProfile.apply runs over the scene (a forced web variant on the desktop skips it: it sets global LOD and snow scales). Census (RenderingServer, WSL + Xvfb software GL, the Compatibility renderer): the desktop variant (shots_menus --desktop) 1079 draw calls on the main screen with the UI, 1039 in shot A, 728 in shot B (all under 1200), 10 lights of which 2 shadowed, 525 meshes and 133 multimeshes; the web variant at 1280x720 819 draw calls, 1 shadowed light, 4 zombies. Load: 1.94 s headless on this PC from MenuScene._ready to the first frame with the zombies posed (the build 1.47 s: the set 1.03 s with the landscape, the zombies 0.21 s once MenuScene generates only the 7 clips it plays instead of the whole ZombieClips library (0.57 s before), the squad 0.22 s); the Terms & Conditions hub starts a threaded load of menu_inn.tscn (MenuScene.preload_set), so from the hub it is shorter still; software GL under WSL takes 8 s and is no measure. The UI: the wordmark 140 px top left over TERMS & CONDITIONS in letter-spaced Special Elite and the tagline; a column of MenuKit.menu_link buttons (Special Elite 40 px at 1080p, letter-spaced caps; 36 in the sub-columns) behind a 2 px violet rule: PLAY (SOLO = the survivor, then the map cards; ONLINE = menu_lobby.gd, retitled "Online", logic untouched; CAMPAIGN = the survivor, then the campaign card), MUSTER, ARCHIVE, EXTRAS (DOSSIERS, SERVICE RECORD, CHALLENGES, CREDITS, TEST RANGE), SETTINGS, QUIT (TERMS & CONDITIONS = back to the hub, which stays the first screen; QUIT TO DESKTOP, not in a browser). Hover is focus (the mouse grabs focus): the label slides 12 px right while a violet glow and left border come up (one tweened StyleBoxFlat per link) and the typewriter ticks; a press stamps; a column change whooshes; the three sounds (ui_tick, ui_stamp, ui_whoosh) are synthesized by gen_sfx.gd (-- --only=menu; in a full regeneration they run last so every older sound stays byte-identical). Columns and every MenuScreen come in with a 0.25 s cross-fade and a 20 px slide (MenuKit.transition_in / transition_out) over a darker shade; Esc or pad B goes back a column, and on the main column opens QUIT's; up and down wrap. Readability: everything is laid out at 1920x1080 and the project's canvas_items stretch scales it, so a 1280x720 browser window shows the same layout at 2/3 (shots at both sizes); nothing on the new screens is under 15 px at 1080p. Map select: a 4-column grid of MapCards (gravelight/ui/map_cards.gd: a 16:9 preview, the kicker (MAP 0 ... FERNFLUG · ACT IV), the name, the date line from TIMELINE, BEST n, EGG DONE / NOT DONE / NO EGG; locked cards stamped LOCKED with their unlock rule), the campaign as the first card. Previews: gravelight/ui/map_previews/<map_id>.png at 960x540 from the new capture set u09_menu_previews (capture.gd gained a "maps" mode: each camera loads its own map, no bots, every CanvasLayer hidden, a free camera at a given eye position or at the map's own start view (player: the player camera pulled back and up)), rendered through WSL and copied by tools/menu_previews.py. Loading: LoadingScreen (gravelight/ui/loading_screen.gd / .tscn): the preview full screen under a grade, the act and the name in the stencil, the date, one lore line for the map and one tip (data/gravelight/tips.json: 30 tips checked against the game's numbers, e.g. bleed-out is 45 s, ammo boxes are paid refills; lore_lines for all 7 maps), a violet bar from ResourceLoader.load_threaded_request, at least 1.2 s on screen, `await MapPacks.ensure(scene)` first (T-139; the fetch's progress on the same bar, back to the menu with a message if it fails); used by MenuCharacter._start and Campaign.travel (solo), which no longer await MapPacks themselves since the loading screen does; Net's online start is untouched (its peers' load sync is C3's). Game over: the ledger, one line per player on a cream ruled page (kills, headshots, Marke kills, Scrip earned, downs, revives) from GameState.player_stats (new, bumped by the same signals, revives from player_revived), which MapRoot ships inside the game-over stats ("players") so a co-op client shows the host's ledger; "The account is closed." is typed out a key at a time. Pause: the wordmark masthead, a violet rule, letter-spaced tabs, tips from tips.json, hint text 13 -> 16 px. Settings: T-139's preset list (GLSettings.preset_order, "Browser" on the web) kept as merged, plus an auto-detect line under it (GLSettings.auto_detect_preset with the adapter and the running renderer). MenuKit.keep_centred now writes the margins only when they change (an unsized parent fed it back into a stack overflow). Tests: test_menu_scene (9: the exported set, desktop counts and budgets, the web variant halves the zombies, the lane loops and the camera cuts, the hooks read the profile, the Fernflug flag from the ending, the cards' egg status and progress, the main menu's columns and ONLINE), test_tips (5: 30 tips, lore lines per map, the picks, the loading screen per map, the ledger rows); smoke_menus (the menu runs 20 s headless: 6 walkers, the squad, two loops round the lane, the camera drifts, all 10 screens open and close by ui_down / ui_accept / ui_cancel events, Esc to QUIT's column and back). Acceptance captures: gravelight/tools_src/shots_menus.tscn through tools/wsl_godot.sh (21 PNGs in morning/screenshots/u09_menus/: 01_main, 02_play, 03_extras, 04_map_select, 05_campaign, 06_online_home, 07_online_browse, 08_online_room (the mock relay: a hosted lobby with bots), 09_muster, 10_archive, 11_settings_video, 12_scene_a, 13_scene_b, 14_mondlicht, 15_loading, 16_pause, 17_game_over at 1920x1080 and 01_main_720, 04_map_select_720, 11_settings_video_720, 15_loading_720; each looked at and fixed where wrong: a solid lavender focus box (the link's shadow filled the rect), the second row of cards under the fold, the Moon behind the gable, the loading screen unsized under a CanvasLayer, the HUD over the game-over page, Settings naming the project's renderer instead of the running one). Not done here: the FPS bench at minimum spec (it needs a GPU window, the owner's PC only with the owner's OK) and a Forward+ draw-call count (WSL renders the Compatibility renderer only; the desktop variant counted there with --desktop is the proxy).

T-148, 2026-11-01: Fernflug Act III (Groom Lake, map04c) rebuilt to the bar (UPDATE 09 section 5, docs/MAPS_3_4_REBUILD.md 5.4; branch u09-5-act3). An art pass only: every colliding box, wall and ramp line in gen_map04c.gd is textually unchanged (diffed), and the regenerated scene's 20 baked navmeshes and 11 nav links are byte-identical to master's, so walkable area, door costs, burrow spawns, launch points, the MPs' edge spawns (T-142), the Range Finance Office and the Ledger Lager machine (T-141) did not move; only non-colliding boxes changed (the hull's ceiling now open over the bay, the hangar's and the motor pool's flat roofs replaced by a pitched shingle roof on king-post trusses and a lean-to carport, Salt_N split so the silo shaft is open to the sky, a salt lid over the lab, the box mountains replaced), plus `hide` / `mat_rules` entries (the wing, tower-mast and G_E/G_N/G_S_E colliders draw nothing; their look is dressing). The pieces are static functions in the new gravelight/tools_src/detail_map04c.gd (gen_map04_lib.gd untouched): generator-scripted SurfaceTool meshes rather than Blender, because they are one source of truth with the generator and need no binary asset: the Kranich lofted on its belly over the spawn room (a blended centre body cut open at the bay, NACA wings swept back so nothing hangs over the walkable salt north of z -4.15, the starboard wing bent up at the tip, the port stub and the torn port wing with rib and spar stubs, intakes), the parabolic radar dish on a four-legged lattice (proportioned struts), the canvas truck covers, the white star (the period's generic vehicle star; markings are only the star, ARMY and a number), the ridge rings; primitives for the rest: three 1940s cargo trucks and a utility car (no CC0 truck that reads as 1940s exists in Kenney / Quaternius / Poly Haven, whose own list has no vehicles, so the trucks are built; logged as the one asset-sourcing deviation), the bomber on jacks filling its collider (the outer engines gone, nothing below the wing's 2.25 m outside the collider), Hale's glass office with mullions, Dee's cage (the counter moved south of her radio; the bank window clear), the generator set in an open-sided shed, the fuel tank and drums, the LANTERN rocket (a kept group named Rocket, so fx: launch still lifts it) and its gantry with lift cage and swing arms, framed glass fronts on every specimen-room jamb, the A-frame hoist over the stone's pit, range markers, scrub, tyre tracks, berm revetments. The sky is a real `shader_type sky` (gravelight/world/map04/shaders/desert_sky.gdshader: the blue hour, indigo zenith, the dawn glow in the east, the earth-shadow belt in the west, twinkling stars, cirrus; Compatibility-safe) with two ridge rings inside the 400 m camera far plane and the salt carried to them by far cards; the sun sits 6 degrees over the eastern ridge as the lake bed's shadowed key (2 cascades, 80 m). The salt is a world-space playa shader (crack polygons, distance-faded) that also draws the black salt (two scorch slots: 44 m round the wreck, the core's crater), because alpha-blended ground cards were not drawn over the salt in the Compatibility captures. The wreck's moving fire is visible: WreckFireFx follows GroomController.fire_point() with flames, smoke, a screen-texture heat shimmer and the zone's shadowed key; DustStormFx follows a new one-line getter GroomController.dust_active() (sweep cards of blowing sand, grit round the camera, fog_sky_affect raised), so the storm's timing, fog tween and burrow packs are the controller's as before. Lights: zone meta on every light (from the zone boxes, the sun B_LAKEBED), one shadowed key per zone (A the fire, B the sun, C Hale's desk lamp, D the generator lamp, E the shack door lamp, F the stone, G the gantry flood), visible fixtures for all of them; Hale's and Dee's lamps from T-110 kept. Budget (C4): the lib's 22 m batching split ~45 dressing materials into 785 batches (2886 draw calls at the B_marker camera), so the generator bakes the dressing itself per material, shadow flag and 60 m cell (333 batches) and small dressing casts no shadow; census (software GL, web profile) before 1562 draw calls / 238k primitives peak, after 2287 / 381k (B_marker / A_outside), shadowed per zone max 1; the web 200k triangle target is not met (master was already 238k) and is left for the web pass. Ambience: gravelight/tools_src/gen_audio_desert.py (owned, synthesized on gen_audio.py's DSP: seven zone beds and four one-shot families, keys merged into audio.json) and data/gravelight/ambience/map04c.json (ZoneAmbience's schema; inert until ZoneAmbience lands). The soak command in the work order needs `--scenario=fernflug` (the script's scenario is a flag; the bare word ran the Map 0 default).
T-145, 2026-11-01: Totenhafen rebuilt to the Crossroads/Kaltenbrunn bar (UPDATE 09 section 5, docs/MAPS_3_4_REBUILD.md 3 / 4.1 / 5.1; branch u09-5-totenhafen), plus ZoneAmbience for every map. An art pass: no collider, navmesh, door, timing or prompt moved (checked: the 22 baked navigation meshes and every NavigationLink3D in map02.tscn are byte-identical to master, and so are its 358 collision boxes; all new pieces are dressing).
