The last run stopped at the limit with 7,035 left on the counter, which is the reserve plus about 2,000 of lag margin; the next run resumes at bake_state.json (`fp.c3.*` Fine Print chapter 3, then Bolt & Sprocket). **Left:** 158,596 billed characters (Fine Print 101,311 over 1,639 lines, Bolt & Sprocket 57,242 over 728 lines), about 70,000 of the counter, so one Creator month after the reset on 2026-12-07 is enough (Pro is not needed); morning/NEEDS_DEVIN.md has it. **Problems:** (1) five lines are stage directions only (REI_HEART_END, LSL_REC_DRUMMER_01, CSN_HUM_01, CSN_WHISPER_01, HAM_CATCH_01: text like "[faint giggle under the drum]"), and the API answers 400 input_text_empty after removing the tags; the bake skips them and they keep their TTS take (no credit spent). They want sound effects (`tools/voice.sh elevenlabs sfx`) or a spoken placeholder text; I did not edit the line texts. (2) No silence, wrong-language or failure pattern anywhere. **Spot checks** (ffmpeg ebur128 and ffprobe on every new file, per group): duration 0.5-55 s, characters per second plausible except very short grunts, loudness median -17.6 LUFS in every group (range -22.6 to -14.9 for the quiet and shouted lines, as in the auditions), true peak at most -0.4 dB except one squad line (WRN_M4C_CRATE_01 at +0.3 dB, 104 characters, 6.5 s, a shout with a tight limiter; left as is), no silent files. Manifests: every new row is `source: ai_eleven`, `ai: true` with the TTS row kept under `tts`; there are no human takes yet and none were touched; the five stage-direction lines have no baked file at all (no TTS row either; some sit under `missing`). Test fixes: tests/voice/conftest.py swaps AI rows back to their kept TTS rows in the sandbox copy (the voice tests start from the TTS state), and three GUT tests (test_vo_human, test_vo_voices) now accept ai_eleven / elevenlabs rows. After the bake: `tools/voice.sh test` 51/51, GUT test_vo 41/41 passing, smoke vo PASS.

T-203, 2026-11-07, UPDATE 11 FINISH and the owner's "the game must load into the menu": the owner's run of Play.bat stopped in the cutscenes ("I can't even get into a menu. It's just cutscenes"); no Windows crash record existed and the log ended mid-load with no backtrace, and every reproduction (off-screen GPU, the owner's profile and settings, muted real audio) reached the menu at 240 FPS, so the likeliest cause was a long first-run freeze closed by hand while three smoke shards loaded the CPU. The calls: (1) **Play.bat opens the CLAUDE ZOMBIES menu** (`res://gravelight/ui/main_menu.tscn`, logging to `logs/game_last.log`); the old button launcher is `DevLauncher.bat`. (2) **Cutscenes play only in the campaign** (the owner's rule): the first campaign opens with LAZARUS SATURDAY, FOURTH DAY and GRAVELIGHT ONE, later campaigns skip the prologue; solo and online play none (`standalone_rule: never`); the menu no longer autoplays the prologue. (3) **Extras > Cutscenes** replays every cutscene (MenuArchive gallery). (4) **A Windows exe** (`tools/build_windows.sh`; export feature `cz_direct` boots straight to the CLAUDE ZOMBIES menu); an off-screen boot of the exe reached the menu in 5.5 s. (5) A cutscene over a match holds the match's own voice lines (GLVO.cinema_hold). (6) `.bat` files stay CRLF (.gitattributes). Follow-ups the owner asked for, run as workers: the Russian speakers re-voiced in English with their Russian voices, the Russian takes kept under a language store (T-204); subtle room acoustics on every voice line (T-205); a Fable pass on the cutscene animation (T-206).
T-204, 2026-11-08: the Russian-speaking cast speaks English with a Russian accent (the owner: "make the people speak English in a Russian accent instead of the Russian language; keep the Russian files in case someone wants to play in Russian"). **What moved:** all 85 Russian / Church Slavonic lines (SER 16 with the 7 diary read-alouds, VOL 16, SVR 29, RTN 16, BRG 8). In docs/gravelight/VO_SCRIPT.md their rows now read `<ru>Russian</ru> English`: extract_vo.py keeps the Russian out of the line (no `speech` field; the English is both what is said and the subtitle) and writes it to the language store data/gravelight/vo/lang/ru.json (id -> speech, subtitle, and `audio`: the Russian take's whole manifest row). The 85 baked Russian OGGs moved to gravelight/audio/vo_lang/ru/<speaker>/<id>.ogg (git renames, LFS), so nothing Russian was deleted. VOManager has `voice_language` ("" = English; "ru" plays the vo_lang take where one exists, subtitles stay English); no menu sets it yet (a later language option only has to set it). The German lines (REI/PA in STAUB, the U-boat radio) keep `<speech>` and stay German. **The English:** the existing subtitles were already natural English with a light Russian turn of phrase, so 72 lines say exactly their subtitle; 13 were reworded for speaking (SER_LZS_01 "The keys I won't give you...", _02, _03, _04, _09; VOL_LZS_02, _03, _05; BRG_LZS_01; RTN_CLOSE_04 "Hundred and eighteenth Rifle"; SVR_M1_01 "By the truckload") and SER_LZS_08's direction became "[quietly, half-sung]" (the old "[sung under the breath]" mapped to [breathing heavily]). The Church Slavonic inscription and troparion are sung / read in English too (the owner asked for English; the Slavonic is in the store). No caricature: no "da", no "comrade" that the Russian did not have. The diary rows carry their delivery tag on both languages. **The bake:** the same five ElevenLabs library voices (native Russian speakers) with language `en` (cast.json `language: en`, `accent: ru`), so the accent comes from the voice. No "[Russian accent]" tag: it cannot be checked without listening, v4 may read an unknown tag aloud, and a forced accent risks the caricature the owner does not want; one test line first (SER_LZS_01: 4.18 s for 62 characters, -17.6 LUFS, true peak -1.4 dB, against 3.99 s for the Russian take). No studio direction in any line or prompt (the room comes from the VO bus reverb another worker adds). All 85 baked in four runs in priority order (cutscenes, then the in-match radio and the Returned, then the diary), plus BRG_LZS_01 and VOL_LZS_05 a second time, shortened so they no longer run past the next cutscene line (the Russian takes fitted there): 10,393 characters billed, the account counter 6,380 -> about 1,880 left of 131,000 (0.43 per billed character as before; reset 2026-12-07). Nothing left for December from this change. The run cleared the main bake_state.json (the bake saves an empty state when it finishes); it was put back (resume at fp.c3.outro.09). **Checks by numbers (no listening):** every take English, 48 kHz mono, 0.7-55.8 s, 8.6-17.5 characters per second except the pause-heavy short lines (Mama., Cold. Cold..., the breathing seal line), loudness -15.1 to -21.0 LUFS (the whispers and diaries quietest), true peak at most -0.9 dB, no inner silence over 0.9 s; durations within about 10 % of the Russian takes. The four cutscene lines that overrun the next line's start (BRG_LZS_02/_03/_08, SER_LZS_06) overran by the same amount in Russian. Tests: test_vo_speech (the German speech lines, the store and the voice_language switch), tests/voice/test_extract_vo (the `<ru>` tag, the store's files), test_elevenlabs (the cast speaks en, the jobs are English).
T-205, 2026-11-08: room acoustics for the voices (the owner: "make sure it doesn't sound like they're in a studio ... the environment that they're actually in. Not too obscene"; branch u11-vo-room-acoustics). **At runtime, not in the bake:** every take (ElevenLabs, TTS, recorded, future) gets its room in the game, so nothing is re-baked and one take can sound in several places. `VORoomAcoustics` (gravelight/world/vo_room_acoustics.gd, `GLVO.rooms`) puts a named AudioEffectEQ6 and AudioEffectReverb (dry 1.0) on the existing VO bus and sets them from one preset of data/gravelight/vo_rooms.json at a time (0.8 s crossfade; a dry preset disables both). **Order:** a playing LAZARUS cutscene (CinemaReel, per shot: its `vo_room` field, else its set's `cinema_sets` entry), else the local player's ZoneVolume zone (the boxes ZoneAmbience uses; between boxes the last zone holds), else the map's default, else dry (menus, the Archive, test_range). **Presets** (wet 0.04 to 0.15; the test caps wet at 0.18 and EQ at 9 dB): room_wood (the inn's taproom and upstairs, barracks, Kantine, the train set), room_plaster, barn_wood (the stable), cellar_stone (cellars, the crypt set), church_stone (the Salt Chapel), mine_tunnel, hall_large, bunker_concrete, dugout (the FOURTH DAY post), outdoor (every map's outside), plane_cabin (the Kranich, the C-47 set: tight, metallic, lows and top down for the engines), sub_hull (the U-boat), suit_radio (Mondlicht's vacuum yard: EQ only), dry / radio. **Voices outside the room** play on a new VODry bus (same `vol_vo`, to Master): fx.json speakers whose chain is not `clean` (the Operator's tannoy, the radios, the PAs, the phonograph, the megaphone, the Cousins' baked room; test-synced), PAUL's PA lines and lines cued [radio] / [intercom] / [telephone] / [static] / [tannoy] / [pa] ...; the bake already band-limits them, so no second filter and no room. The game has no Narrator voice (cards). World scripts that play a line themselves (inn opening and survivors, the trapped pilot, tellers, extra people) take `GLVO.bus_for_line(id)`. 3D lines keep their attenuation (the room is after it, on the bus). **Setting:** `vo_room_acoustics` (default on), Settings > Audio > Room acoustics. **Not done:** Area3D reverb buses (the zone boxes cover 2D lines too); no per-shot overrides written (each set is one space). Headless only, nothing listened to: the owner should compare Map 0's taproom and cellar and GRAVELIGHT ONE with the setting on and off. Test: test_vo_rooms (9).
T-206, 2026-11-08, the LAZARUS cutscenes' polish pass (branch u11-9-cine-polish; the owner: "I like the cutscene for the most part, but a lot of the animations are just a little bit off"; the shot-by-shot notes are docs/CUTSCENE_POLISH.md). **How it was looked at:** no GPU run. A new headless probe (gravelight/tools_src/probe_cinema_pose.tscn) plays a cutscene through CinemaReel and logs, three times a shot, the lens and every person's distance, off-axis angle, in-frame state, ground speed, head and hand heights, held props and anything of a body within 0.4 m of the lens; the read-through of the stage code was checked against it before and after. It found the lens inside bodies four times (the colonel's raised hands in FOURTH DAY 3, Volkov's hip in LAZARUS 22, a soldier's shoulder in FOURTH DAY 10, the back of Volkov's head 0.45 m in front of the lens in LAZARUS 19), the subject of a shot out of its frame three times (Serafim's hands 34 degrees off a 35-degree lens in 10, Serafim 92 degrees off in the belfry crane of 12, Volkov 78 degrees off in the aftermath two-shot of 17, the aircraft's centre 45 degrees off a 40-degree lens in GRAVELIGHT ONE 7), the OP's roof through the officers' caps, three groups facing the wrong way (the cordon facing the lens, the kitchen soldiers already facing the graves they are meant to turn to, the mill crew and Reinholt with their backs to the hopper and the gauges), hands 25-35 cm short of what they held (the vessel, the dial, the yoke), men spawned inside the truck and walking through its bonnet, and Volkov sliding down the crypt stair at 0.17 m/s. **The puppet (CinemaPerson) is where most of the "off" lived, so that is where the fix is:** (1) pose changes blend instead of snapping (hands glide in about 0.3 s, a kneel folds or rises in about 0.4 s, a head turns in 0.3 s; a teleport snaps, since a cut should show the new pose on its first frame); (2) a walk of its own: the stride follows the speed (0.45-0.85 m a step walking, up to 1.3 at a run) and the hip swing is set so the foot's arc equals the stride, so a planted foot stays put against the ground (the puppet's fixed small swing over a fixed 0.7 m stride skated everyone); (3) `look_toward(point)` aims the head within a neck's range, and the stages aim speakers at listeners; (4) `hand_l` / `hand_r` chest-local hand overrides a stage sets or tweens (the handset passed hand to hand, a hand to a cap, a sign of the cross, the throttles and the overhead switches, a hand on the dial, hands on the yoke), `hold_width`, two poses (`raise`, `binoculars`); (5) `walk_to(target, m/s)` turns a person to face the way then moves at a walking speed (the old sine position tweens peaked at 1.6x their mean and never turned anyone, so people strafed), `turn_to` turns the short way (the old rotation tweens went the long way from 180 to 0), and `reset_motion()` kills a walk still running so a teleport is not dragged back along its path. **The stages** then re-block the beats the shot lists describe and the build had left static (Volkov looks at the kneeling road and away, reads his paper and looks up, glances at the brigade head on his line, turns to the man who is actually there, carries his own lantern down the stair and raises it to the letters, kneels within reach of the vessel and works at the wax, rises "too fast" on the cut, walks out last; the brigade come down the truck's far side and round its nose, haul the rope facing the tower with their hands on it and let it run with the bell, run out of the porch; the colonel lowers his binoculars, takes the handset and asks with his face; the soldiers turn head first; a cap, a cross, a watch; Selwyn's hands do everything right; the passengers brace) and fix the stage geometry (the OP roof 23 cm higher under a lintel, the yokes within a seated reach, the rope from the bell to the haulers' hands, the slit's light in the slit). **Data (cutscenes_lazarus.json):** eleven camera corrections (listed per shot in CUTSCENE_POLISH.md), seven new effect hooks (`reverse`, `colonel_asks`, `faces`, `lean_window`, `watch`, `brace`; `lean_window` is T-194's own effect, left unhooked when a standing passenger blocked the glass: the lens now sits beside him, past his shoulder), one ease (`in_sine` to `smooth` on the push into the vessel: a push should settle, not accelerate into the cut) and one line time (SVR_FTD_02 from 0.4 to 2.0 s, after the handover it answers; the reading-speed overlap test still passes). No line, card, length or story beat changed; the linear tracking shots (the trench, the road, the glide's drift) stay linear on purpose: they come in from black or a dip and go out on a cut, and an ease would make them stop. **Not changed, deliberately:** lines.json, the VO files, the audio bus (other workers), the quests, gameplay. **Known limits** (CUTSCENE_POLISH.md, last section): the bodies have no mouth shapes but Selwyn's and the squad's, so the Russian lines have no lip flap; a kneel is a blend, not a get-up clip; feet follow a stair's slope with no step IK; hands are a closed fist at a point (no fingers on the dial or the yoke); the stage beats run in real seconds, so CinemaReel's fast speeds (the smokes at 10-12x) leave the walks behind the clock, which is harmless there and right at the game's speed 1. Measured: check_scripts 824/0, GUT test_cinema* 44/44 (1,820 asserts), smoke_cinema_lazarus PASS (all five play and skip, the campaign start plays the three, Kaltenbrunn solo none); the probe at speed 1 shows no body part within 0.4 m of a lens in any shot and every named subject inside its frame.

T-212, 2026-11-08, UPDATE 12 section 6 (branch u12-6-aircraft): background aircraft match the Soviet front. **Audit (Map 0 `map00_ambience.json`, Kaltenbrunn `map01_ambience.json`, both through MapAmbience/SkyFlight):** before, bomber streams of 7-14 of the code-built four-engined twin-tail "bomber", fighter pairs of the single-engined "fighter" (the second chases the first with tracer), the round-6 (Kaltenbrunn round-5) plane that is hit and falls (from a bomber stream, or index 0 of a fighter pair), and Kaltenbrunn's round-7 dogfight (two "fighter"s). Out of scope and kept: Totenhafen's round-6 Mosquito low pass (T-154, a different place and story), Fernflug's formation round the Kranich and its bomber flyover (Allied, map04), the menu/exfil/GL1 aircraft and the C-47 (another worker). **After:** bomber streams are Soviet Pe-2 class (twin engine, twin fin, red stars); each fighter pair is a Soviet Il-2 class (index 0, the chased one) with a German night fighter (Bf 110 class, antenna array, index 1) on its tail; Kaltenbrunn's dogfight is the same pair. **The round-6 plane that falls is now a Soviet aircraft:** a bomber-stream faller is a Pe-2, a fighter-pair faller (`_shoot_down(f, 0)`) is the Il-2 (the German chases, so the Soviet falls). Timing, counts, chances, crash bearing/radius, the glow, boom, VO trigger and score (none) are untouched; only meshes changed (`model` keys in the json: `pe2`, `il2_vs_night`; no key = the old meshes, so other maps are unchanged). **Models:** our own CC0 work, built in code like the old ones (PlaneModels, shared ArrayMeshes of a few hundred triangles; at 6.5 m / 3.4 m scale they are silhouettes against the dome), so no third-party asset folder and no credits entry; free downloads would add a licence chain for something seen as a dark shape at 300 m. Soviet red stars are flat red slabs on the wing tips and fin. German aircraft carry no marking at all (ART_FACTIONS: the game draws no national emblem; the Wiederkehr sunburst is for kit and crates). **Sound:** kept (`war_bomber_drone`, `war_fighter_pass`; the sounds are the project's own synthesis and no licensed Soviet-radial/German-twin set exists to add). **Needs the owner:** the SKY_PLANE_DOWN squad lines (baked VO) still read as an Allied crew ("ten men in that thing", "glide it in like ours did", "same bearing we came in on"); with a Soviet plane the crew-of-ours lines no longer fit, so re-writing and re-baking them is a follow-up (baked text, not touched here). Test: test_u12_aircraft.
T-210, 2026-11-08: UPDATE 12 section 4, phone motion capture and lip sync for the in-engine cutscenes (branch u12-4-mocap; contract C1: tools/mocap/, docs/MOCAP_GUIDE.md, assets/user_supplied/mocap/, gravelight/world/lipsync*; plus gravelight/world/mocap/ for the runtime). **One list drives everything.** data/gravelight/mocap/shots.json names every filmable shot of the squad's in-engine scenes: GRAVELIGHT ONE (7 shots), the Map 0 and Kaltenbrunn exits (exfil_m0_m1, exfil_m1_m2: the campaign's "Kaltenbrunn ending"), the Requiem egg ending (requiem_ending), Totenhafen's exit, Fernflug's two crossings with people in frame and KUENDIGUNG (cancel_ending). That is 27 shots and 100 takes, 775 s of motion (527 s high priority). Each shot has its roles, the phone spot, the marks, what each role does, the props, its lines, `root` and `priority`. The report, the build, the guide (`tools/mocap.sh guide` writes docs/MOCAP_GUIDE.md's tables between markers; a pytest fails when they are stale) and the runtime lookup all read it. Not filmed (listed in the guide with the reason): the Letzte Fahrt dive and the rocket launch (nobody is directed into frame), the London window and the renewal insert (camera only), and the four history scenes (§3 makes them archive reels). **Roles.** A take is per shot per role, `<shot>_<role>.fbx|.bvh`. A role is a character, a C-47 seat (`seat1..4`, because the passengers are whoever plays, and the window-seat action belongs to the seat), a group slot (`slot1..4`), or `any`. The runtime tries the character first, then the seat or slot, then `any`. One performer can play everyone, one take after another. The KUENDIGUNG key shot plays only for whoever turned the key, plus Jakub (`mocap_only`). **Pipeline** (Python + numpy; Blender 5.1 headless only to open an FBX). `blender_extract.py` and the pure-Python BVH reader give the same "motion": world joints in Godot's frame. On the sample the BVH through Blender matched the Python reader exactly, and an FBX round trip came within 1.7 degrees after a one-frame bake lead. `mocaplib.py` does the rest. **Bone names:** aliases for Mixamo, Rokoko, DeepMotion `_JNT`, UE and CMU; the chest is the neck's nearest spine. **Retarget:** each rig bone takes the source bone's world turn away from its rest, after a per-bone rest alignment (our arms-down rest onto the source's T-pose by bone direction), with the facing at frame 0 mapped to -Z and the hips scaled by leg length. **Resample** to 30 fps. **Trim:** 1.0 s after the slate clap (both hands within 12 cm in the first 5 s), else the end of a still start, else 0; a sidecar `<file>.json` {"start", "cutoff", "lock"} overrides. **Smooth:** zero-phase 2nd-order Butterworth, 6 Hz on the rotations and 5 Hz on the hips. A one-euro filter was rejected because a cutscene is offline, so zero phase costs nothing. **Ground:** 5th percentile of the lower sole at y = 0. **Foot lock:** contact = sole under 5 cm and ankle under 0.35 m/s for 3+ frames; the ankle is pinned where it landed, eased over 4 frames, by two-bone IK in the knee's own plane, with the foot keeping its world turn. Results on the synthetic walk: slide 0.19 to 0.03 m/s, jitter down 65 %. `mocap_import.gd` (Godot headless) saves one AnimationLibrary per scene, assets/ours/mocap/<scene>/mocap_<scene>.res (animations `<shot>_<role>`, a Hips position track plus 17 rotation tracks, about 30-45 KB a clip). The build writes SOURCE.txt and LICENSE.txt ("Ours") there for the licence gate. Raw captures stay in the git-ignored assets/user_supplied/ (friends' recordings are personal), and the built clips are committed. **Runtime (an additive hook).** One line at the end of CinemaDirector._begin_shot calls `MocapStage.on_shot`. The scene comes from the definition's `mocap_scene`, else the CinemaReel cut or ExfilCutscene playing it. The two endings gained only dictionary keys: `mocap_scene` and per-shot `mocap`, because their shot count varies with the burned cities. The shot key is `mocap`, else `shot_NN`. The actors are the cast's RemoteBody stand-ins and a reel stage's CinemaPersons. A clip plays through a `MocapModifier` (a SkeletonModifier3D on the stand-in's PersonRig skeleton), so it simply overrides the procedural pose solver while it exists, and the stand-in animation is back the moment it goes. With no library or no clip nothing happens; today there are no captures, so every cutscene plays exactly as before. The clock is the director's (skip, speed and fast-forward stay in step), and the last frame holds. `root: mocap` keeps the body where the shot began plus the take's own path, whatever the cast does with the node; `cast` plays in place, looped, and the cast walks the node (the long walks: nobody can walk 9 m in a phone frame). A same-key next shot continues the clip (the key's two-shot). The RemoteBody's gun is hidden while a clip plays (it rides the puppet's hands, not the rig's) and comes back after. **Lip sync.** Rhubarb Lip Sync 1.14.0 (MIT, Daniel S. Wolf) is downloaded at run time into git-ignored tools/dl_rhubarb/ (an 85 MB zip with its own .gdignore; not committed). It runs on each line's ElevenLabs take: PocketSphinx with the line's text as the dialog for English, the phonetic recognizer otherwise. The cues go to data/gravelight/vo/lipsync/<ID>.json, compact JSON with the duration set to the take's length; all 108 cutscene lines were made with Rhubarb, and `--all` covered every baked Gravelight line: 2,519 in all, about 4.9 MB. 631 lines used PocketSphinx: the English cutscene lines and the first in-match batch. The other 1,888 used the phonetic recognizer: the non-English lines and the rest of the in-match lines, because PocketSphinx takes several seconds a line and phonetic about one. Every file's duration is within 0.1 s of the manifest's. If Rhubarb can't be fetched or fails, the cues come from the take's loudness (20 ms RMS → X/B/C/D) and `source` says so. FaceDriver (the only code change outside my folders besides the hook) loads a line's cues on `vo_line_started` and moves `jaw_open` by them plus `smile` at half weight for the wide shapes, with a 60 ms coarticulation ease. A line without cues keeps the old syllable flap. A head with no blend shapes (ZombieBody.build_dressed bodies) gets `LipSyncBones`, a modifier that opens the Jaw bone and lifts the chin a touch on open vowels (the brow beat). **Found:** the jaw opens with a **-X** turn. test_mocap compares Dutch's `jaw_open` shape against the bone turn, and +X moves the jaw the other way. The MZ zombie clips key the jaw +X for the slack jaw (zombie_clips `_rest` and the gape); that is worth a look in the zombie art, and I didn't change it. Dressed cutscene people (Volkov, Serafim) have no speaker code on their FaceDriver, so they don't lip-sync yet; their scenes are becoming reels anyway. **The apps** (checked 2026-11-08): Rokoko Vision Starter: 30 s of Vision processing a month, FBX, commercial use allowed. DeepMotion Freemium: 60 s a month, FBX/BVH/MP4, personal non-commercial only. QuickMagic Free: 50 V-coins, 30 s per video, FBX only, no commercial licence. No free tier covers 775 s, so the owner chooses a paid month (NEEDS_DEVIN). **Tests:** pytest tests/mocap: 20 tests, including the BVH and FBX-through-Blender end-to-end retargets, foot slide under 5 cm/s and under 30 % of the raw, jitter halved, the filter passing 1 Hz and cutting 12 Hz, the missing-file report, the build, shots.json's completeness, the guide being current, every cutscene line's cues ending within 0.1 s of its take, the loudness fallback, and a live Rhubarb run. GUT test_mocap: 13 tests: the clip becomes an Animation, the lookup misses cleanly and walks the roles, the modifier's pose, root motion holding against a moving node, in-place looping, a real GRAVELIGHT ONE reel playing a seat's clip and falling back on the next shot and for the other seats, scene ids and shot keys, shots.json against the cutscene data, the cues against the manifest, the mouth shapes, FaceDriver following the cues, the bone fallback, and the jaw's direction.

T-207, 2026-11-08: UPDATE 12 section 1, the look (branch u12-1-bible; U12 contract C2). **The direction** is the owner's and is written into docs/ART_BIBLE.md Part A: desaturated war-film realism, grim and stylized, closer to Black Ops 1 zombies than to photoreal; muted greys, browns and cold blues under heavy fog; Nachtglas violet the only bright supernatural colour and blood red the second; Bolt & Sprocket and The Fine Print stay cartoon. The bible now carries the palette with hex swatches (the neutrals, the practicals, the two accents, three swatches per map), display-luma value ranges for night / dusk / day / vacuum / the reels, the fog density ranges per kind of space, the grain / vignette / edge-softness / chromatic-aberration numbers for section 2's post stack, the violet-and-red rules (where, and how much of the frame: 5% in a normal frame, up to 25% in the Core and on Mondlicht, 0% on the reels), the material rules (roughness floor 0.45 world / 0.35 metal / 0.25 gun metal, no clean plastic, grime never 0, albedo saturation under 0.30), the lighting rules (warm practicals against cold ambient, nights at 0.7-0.9x ambient, readability as a hard rule) and a do/don't sheet pointing at our own captures; the old asset contract (shader, scale, budgets, naming, readability, how to add) is Part B, unchanged except where Part A tightens it. **One grade per map, data not code:** `data/look/<map_id>.json` (the MapRoot map_ids plus `menu`, `cinema_reel`, `_web`), applied by `gravelight/world/look/look_grade.gd` (class LookGrade, Environment-only: tonemap, exposure, white, the adjustments, a generated 32^3 colour-correction LUT, ambient scale, fog and volumetric fog, glow) from one hook in MapRoot._ready (`LookGrade.attach`), one in MenuScene (`menu`), one in CinemaReel._stage_for (a cutscene stage takes its host map's grade, `cinema_reel` for the archive history scenes lazarus_saturday / fourth_day / staub, `menu` in the theatre) and `look_zones()` on Kaltenbrunn's depth_grade.gd (the file's `surface` / `depths` / `core` zones set what it blends by camera height). Absent keys leave the map's value alone, a missing file leaves the map untouched, `--look=off` turns every grade off (the before frames), the Brightness slider (T-133) and the horror layer's darker night (T-192, now also written as the gl_horror_mult meta) still scale the grade's brightness, and `LookGrade.post_params(map_id)` hands section 2 the post block. **The LUT, not the saturation slider, desaturates:** Godot applies brightness / contrast / saturation before the colour-correction texture, so a plain `adjust.saturation` would grey the Nachtglas too; every map instead sets `mono` 0.4-0.6 and a `keep` list, and the LUT greys everything except a saturated violet (hue 268 +-20 degrees, saturation past 0.3-0.55) and red (0 +-18 degrees). The first pass kept +-34 degrees and Map 0's moonlit snow and Groom Lake's salt came out lavender; the frames narrowed it. The web / Compatibility profile has no colour correction, so `_web.json` drops the LUT pieces and each file's `web` block puts a plain saturation (0.72; 0.15 on Mondlicht) in their place. **Filmic replaces AGX**, with the exposure brought down to match (Map 0 1.3 -> 1.0, Totenhafen and Fernflug 2.0 -> 1.7, Kaltenbrunn per zone 1.4 / 1.5 / 1.45 -> 1.05 / 1.2 / 1.2): Filmic at the old exposures lifted the nights (Map 0's mean went 43 -> 49 in the first pass; the pick is 31) and AGX had been greying the warm practicals' highlights, which the direction wants warm. **The picks, one line per map** (`tools/look/style_frames.sh`; frames in docs/look/frames/, the `u12_look` camera set in capture_cams.json, the same cameras as the menu previews, the Kranich cam moved to the cockpit because the unpowered bomb bay is black in any grade and the Groom Lake cam moved out onto the salt because the old lakebed cam now looks into a hangar): Crossroads Inn, frozen night: Filmic 1.0, contrast 1.16, mono 0.5, split tone #203238 -> #d9c9a8 at 0.3, ambient 0.7x, fog 0.022 #10161f, volumetric 0.030 (mean luma 42 -> 31, the windows and the door lantern the warmest things left). Kaltenbrunn, cold steel over salt and sodium: contrast 1.12, mono 0.4, split tone #22303a -> #d8c6a0 at 0.25, fog 0.012 / 0.020, the three depth zones above (surface mean 60 -> 48). Totenhafen, storm green-grey: Filmic 1.7, contrast 1.12, mono 0.45, split tone #1f2b28 -> #b7c2b3 at 0.35, ambient 0.85x, fog 0.011 #2a3a33, volumetric 0.015 albedo #b8c4bb, the red battle lanterns kept (saturated pixels 33% -> 14%). Thalbach, industrial ochre and soot: Filmic 1.7, contrast 1.1, mono 0.5, split tone #1f1a14 -> #cfa865 at 0.45, ambient 0.85x, fog 0.011 #3a2f26 (the shadows went from blue to brown; saturated pixels 49% -> 6%). Der Kranich, dawn blue and cold aluminium: Filmic 1.7, contrast 1.12, mono 0.45, split tone #1c2736 -> #d4dce8 at 0.3, fog #232c38, the ambient left alone, the bay's red lamps kept. Groom Lake, bleached desert and dust brown: Filmic 1.7, contrast 1.06, mono 0.6 with the violet keep at half (the impeller crater's Nachtglas lights the whole salt flat from that camera; kept in full it was a lavender field, past the 25% rule), split tone #4a3d30 -> #ece0c8 at 0.5, fog colour #5a4e40 and albedo #d8c8b0, the density left to the dust storm. Mondlicht, hard black and white: Filmic 1.7, contrast 1.2, mono 1.0 keeping hue 268 +-36 degrees only, so the amber lamps and the red go grey and the violet strips are the only colour. Menu and the reels, archive black-and-white: saturation 0, contrast 1.16, silver split tone #101214 -> #d6dbe3 at 0.25, black lifted 0.04, grain 0.14-0.16, vignette 0.5, flicker (the menu's saturated pixels 51% -> 4%; the paper UI over it is not graded). **Readability checked** on the `u05 close_zombie` camera (the risen Ganger in the stable at 3.4 m, frames/25_close_zombie_*): still read at 0.7x ambient. **Not done here, for the lead / section 2:** a runtime zone follower for the other maps (`LookGrade.apply(env, map_id, zone)` exists; only Kaltenbrunn blends today), the post block drawn (section 2), the `cinema_reel` grade on the real .ogv reels (section 3 treats the video itself; the grade is for the in-engine stand-ins), and a frame of the prologue stage.

T-213, 2026-11-08, upkeep (branch u12-upkeep-vo): **smoke_vo and the Map 0 SPAWN lines.** Two causes. (a) The ElevenLabs takes (T-201) are much longer, so squad lines queue: smoke_vo's down/revive check window is 8 s -> 30 s after the revive and phase 3's end wait 12 s -> 20 s; its scripted DOOR step is now retryable (the door opens once, so a retry re-emits `door_opened`) instead of waiting for a channel it could not be sure to get. (b) **Real bug: on Map 0 nobody said SPAWN.** `_spawn_lines` scheduled each survivor's SPAWN with `_later`; `_tick_delayed` fires such a line once (after at most 10 s of waiting for a story line) and discards it whether or not it played, and a chatter line (`_submit`) is refused while another chatter line is within the 3.5 s gap and the squad channel (`GROUP_PLAYER`) is busy, then queued with a short expiry. The inn opening's patrons talk through `GLBus.vo_line_started` from ~12 s to ~36 s without using the VO groups, and the squad's own ROUND_START / HEADSHOT / BUY quips filled the player channel meanwhile, so all SPAWN calls were refused or expired in the queue. (The lead's after_story attempt could not work: the opening is not in `GROUP_STORY`, and after_story only holds for that group.) **Fix:** `_later_when_free` (entries with `retry_until`): a SPAWN line waits until `_squad_channel_free()` (no story line, nothing playing or queued on the squad channel, the chatter gap passed, and no node of the new group `inn_opening` in `Phase.RUNNING`), for up to 60 s, then is said once (chance and cooldown rules still apply as before). `InnOpening` joins the group `inn_opening` in `_ready`. Opening text and timings untouched. **SKY_PLANE_DOWN (T-212 follow-up):** the plane that falls is Soviet, so four lines are rewritten in VO_SCRIPT.md (the source), lines.json re-extracted, the slot checklists updated: PEG_01 ("Red star on that wing, and it's burning."), DEA_01 ("...glide it in. Whoever he is, let somebody be alive..."), DEA_02 ("a crew of three in that thing, maybe four", a Pe-2 has three), WRN_01 ("short of their own lines"). DUT_01/02, PEG_02 and WRN_02 read neutrally and stay baked as they were. The four were re-baked with ElevenLabs: 486 characters billed (account counter moves about half of that), so about 1,300 of the ~1,800 left until 2026-12-07 remain; nothing is left for the December bake from this change. The main checkout's bake_state.json was restored after the bake (the resume pointer stays at fp.c3.outro.09). **smoke_map00** never hung: it printed `[smoke] PASS`/`FAIL` but no `RESULT` line, which tools/run_smokes.sh greps for; it now prints `[smoke] RESULT PASS|FAIL` as well (exit code unchanged).

T-211, 2026-11-08: UPDATE 12 section 5, GRAVELIGHT ONE as a real C-47 (branch u12-5-c47; contract C1 owns the Map 0 wreck art, the C-47 build and the Map 0 minimap re-bake). **The source.** The Sketchfab models (C47 by manilov.ap CC-BY 4.0 first, KN645 by mgg8 CC0, the two alternates) need the owner's login, so nothing is in assets/user_supplied/u12/c47/ yet: everything is built on a stand-in, and the swap is automatic. The stand-in is not the old box wreck: tools/c47/c47_airframe.py builds a low-poly C-47 / Dakota in headless Blender from the published figures (span 29.0 m, length 19.5 m, the DC-3 planform with the swept leading edge and a 5 deg outer dihedral, the two R-1830 radials in their cowls with two rows of cylinders, 3.5 m three-blade props, the retracted wheels half out of the nacelles, the fin with its dorsal fillet, the tailplane, the astrodome, the D/F loop, the aerial wire, the pitot tubes, the formation and navigation lights) in gen_map00's wreck frame, its section gen_map00's WRECK_RING (half-width 1.44, so ~10 % fuller than the real 2.6 m: the cabin must fit the T-126 hull colliders). Ours, "Ours" licence, credits nobody. **One pipeline for any source** (tools/c47/build_c47.py; `tools/c47/build.sh` picks the first model folder in the inbox, else the stand-in): an imported GLB is joined, turned nose -Z / up +Y by its widest slice (the wing is in the front half), scaled to the 29.0 m span, its cabin fitted to the ring (the shoulder, the keel and the widest point; the wings only shift), its own interior cleared; then for either source the same steps: the vertex colour (R scorch round the fuel fire's points, G soot streaks aft and up off them and dirt, B snow allowance), our markings projected onto the skin by ray casts (data from tools/c47/make_decals.py's atlas: the RAF C1 roundel on the fuselage, B roundels on the upper wings, the fin flash, the fictional serial KQ913 -- the RAF never used Q in a serial, so it is nobody's aircraft -- the individual letter G, GRAVELIGHT ONE stencilled small under the cockpit, NO STEP; no real-world brand on any stencil), then **intact** (the props split into Prop_L / Prop_R with their pivots on the hubs, our intact cabin and cockpit, anchors for the exhaust glow and the lights) and **crashed** from data/gravelight/c47_wreck.json: the skin bisected on the cut planes and every cut edge jittered (torn, not sawn), the pieces split (the starboard wing at the root, the port engine at the firewall, the port wing's outer panel at the joint, the tail behind the split at z 6.3, the starboard tailplane), the blades bent back up to 72 deg toward the tips, the hull's openings punched exactly over gen_map00 WRECK_OPENINGS (the two torn starboard ones subdivided and cut on a noisy outline that only grows outward, with peeled skin petals, stringer stubs and the ribs standing at their sides; the cockpit's side windows and the windscreen clean), the glazing out with a few shards and the windscreen's posts bent, a crack over the roof from the split, dents and a wrinkle band in the skin (inward only, at most 0.12 m, never into the cabin's lining), the nose crushed back and down into the snow it ploughed up (under Selwyn's line of fire, which passes at ~1.4 m over it), the port engine's mount tubes and the firewall left on the nacelle, the hull rolled 8 deg as the map has it, the loose pieces posed and dropped onto the snow, snow drifted against the windward (port: the map's smoke drifts east) side and up under the port wing (the T-080 rule "the wing reaches the snow" is now what you see), the gouge behind with its berms. **The layout calls.** (1) The starboard wing lies in the gouge 20 m behind the fuselage, at the dead tree it struck (it replaces the T-080 WingPanel), half-buried: its collider is a level slab to 0.24 m (under agent_max_climb, so the navmesh walks over it and no unreachable island forms on top; Map 0's intel drops pick random navmesh points), its nacelle two boxes (cowl, rear) so neither top is an 8 m2 island. (2) The port engine is the one T-080 already threw into the north field (34.5, -13.2): the old cylinder art and its box replaced by the model's engine and its fitted box. (3) The port wing's outer panel lies flat in the snow, grey underside up: a drooping panel would have been a 20 deg ramp up onto the wing (a perch, against T-080). (4) The port tailplane, tilted on the rolled tail, gets a level box from the snow to its top edge for the same reason. (5) The tail broke away behind the aft bulkhead (the "split behind the wing" is the starboard opening at z 2.2..4.0 T-126 called the cargo door, now a split with a crack over the roof); the real C-47's cargo door is drawn closed on the port side. (6) The dead co-pilot lies under a parachute's silk on the cockpit floor beside Selwyn, his boots toward the pedals, his helmet set on the silk: in the cockpit, not the cabin, so no player ever walks through him (respect rules, UPDATE 11 section 8); the cutscene's co-pilot is the cinema set's. (7) The paratroop seats are folded up against the walls (0.1 m proud of the collider face) and four thrown loose; the crates lashed by the port door get a collider (CargoStack, 0.7 x 0.75 m in the cabin's aft port corner; T-188). (8) Map 0 has no collapse event: smoke_wreck_collapse is Fernflug III's Kranich (map04c, T-157); it is unchanged and passes. **Colliders** (T-188, collision matches the art): the hull's T-126 boxes are byte-for-byte unchanged (the smoke's paths, capsules and the cockpit's choke); the starboard Wing and Nacelle boxes and the TailPlane box are gone, the port wing is two boxes to the joint fitted to the chord, the port nacelle stub one, the NoseCone one (under the windscreen's sill), and the loose pieces are oriented boxes the build fits to each part in the piece's own axes (data/gravelight/c47_colliders.json, generated with --write-colliders from the stand-in; the real model uses the same boxes, its pieces lie where the layout puts them, so the swap never moves the navmesh). gen_map00 builds them under Geometry/WreckSite; navmesh re-baked (test_map00_art fingerprints updated: only polygons in the crash field x 22..61, z -26..36 and their open-snow neighbours changed). **Bot routes:** the Wreck Loop's notes ask 3.5 m off the wreck; four of its points moved by 0.7-2.3 m round the new tail and wing (routes_map00.json) and tools/c47/route_clearance.py measures 3.3 m at the closest (the port tailplane, the outer panel) against 2.5 m before the move. **In the game** (gravelight/world/c47/): C47Art picks the model (assets/third_party/sketchfab/<id>/{intact,crashed}.glb when built, else assets/ours/world/c47_{intact,crashed}/model.glb), maps the slots once (c47_* onto c47_skin.gdshader: the paint or a real model's own albedo under triplanar wear from the library's olive-over-rust set, scorch, soot, snow on what faces up, both faces drawn so a tear shows the skin's dark back; the markings on c47_decal.gdshader, the same weathering with alpha scissor; mats_* / lib_* through WorldArt), sets the LOD visibility ranges from c47_wreck.json `lod` (LOD0 to 32 m, LOD1 32-65, LOD2 65-120, the interior to 38 m, the impostor beyond 120 m: an 8-view atlas Cycles renders in the build, a Y-billboard picking the view by the camera's bearing). C47Wreck (Map 0, at the wreck's site frame) draws it and the life round it from `fx`: the fuel fire at the starboard wing root, in the torn hole, along the port wing root and the port nacelle, at the wing in the gouge (Props.flames), heat-haze cards over the two big ones (heat_haze.gdshader, screen-texture ripple), sparks, smoke drifting east on the map's wind, and the hull's metal groaning and ticking as it cools (wreck_c47_creak, four takes synthesized by gen_audio_war.py gen_wreck_c47, at random points every 5-13 s). The old procedural wreck (_dress_wreck_hull, the boxes and Label3Ds, LandscapeProps.pinned_cockpit on Map 0, the starboard engine's flame) is gone; the landscape keeps its port-wing flames, cook-offs (the starboard engine's moved to the wing root) and the field's fires. C47Intact (gravelight/world/c47/c47_intact.tscn) is the whole aircraft for the in-flight cutscene: props spinning (prop_rpm, a windmill when engines_on is false), exhaust glow, navigation and formation lights (lights_on), its own copies of the lamp materials; for the cutscene worker's gl1_glide / gl1_c47 sets (not edited here). **Credits:** when a downloaded model is built, tools/c47/intake.py writes its SOURCE.txt (the owner's page link, the credit line) and LICENSE.txt, and build.sh runs assets_manifest.py, so data/credits_art.json and the Credits screen list the artist; the stand-in adds nothing (test_c47_wreck checks both ways). **Proof the import path works without the file:** tools/c47/make_test_source.py re-exports our airframe nose +X, Z-up, in centimetres; build_c47.py --source on it found the span, the nose, the section (half-width 1.452 -> 1.44) and built both versions with every piece in place. Budget: crashed LOD0 44.6k tris with the interior, the ground and the skin doubled (below), LOD1 14.7k, LOD2 6.8k; intact 37.8k with its LODs. **A renderer trap found on the way:** c47_skin with render_mode cull_disabled took the Vulkan pipeline down silently (exit 127, no error) on the RTX 5090 whenever SDFGI was on (found by probing each shader under SDFGI / shadows); the skin is cull_back and the build doubles the exterior skin with flipped faces instead (build_c47.double_side). **Measured** (gravelight/tools_src/shots_c47.tscn under the C3 GPU lock, Forward+ at the default preset, vsync off, 6 s per view; before = master, after = this branch): the inn's front door 110 -> 121 FPS (p95 14.5 -> 10.9 ms, 5,337 -> 5,297 draw calls), the field 129 -> 124 FPS (5,365 -> 6,344 draw calls: the pieces' material surfaces and the fire), inside the cabin 74 -> 119 FPS (the old art's hundreds of boxes are gone); captures in docs/look/c47/{before,after}_{inn_door,field_nw,field_se,field_tail,cabin,cockpit}.jpg with perf_{before,after}.json. Map 0's minimap re-baked (tools/bake_minimap.sh under the lock; its markers and POIs unchanged, the same poi_signature). Tests: test_c47_wreck (the layout's openings equal WRECK_OPENINGS, the model loads with its LODs, interior and impostor and every paint slot mapped, Map 0 draws C47Wreck at the site frame and keeps every hull collider, one debris box per c47_colliders.json entry, all no_mantle, the intact props turn and the lights go dark, the credits follow the source, the creak is registered).

T-209, 2026-11-08, UPDATE 12 section 3 (branch u12-3-reels): the archive-reel cutscenes. **What is a reel:** the three history scenes (LAZARUS SATURDAY, FOURTH DAY, STAUB; GRAVELIGHT ONE and the Moscow card stay in-engine: §4's squad scenes) are cut into reel shots in data/gravelight/cinema_reels.json: 21 / 15 / 13 shots, each a **span of the in-engine timeline** (cutscenes_lazarus.json) with the in-engine shot indices it replaces, a priority, a 4-10 s generation length, a prompt and a must-show note. A reel keeps the in-engine timeline exactly (lines, subtitles, card times, beds, sfx, lengths), so hold to skip, the co-op vote, the host-driven online sync, the Gore setting on the stages and every hook work unchanged; only the picture changes. The black card shots are never generated (our cards). The 1930 respect rule is tightened in the prompts: shot 7 ends before anyone lays a hand on the priest (the in-engine shot 10's hands are not in the reel), the typhus board and the crypt lintel are blank or in shadow (no text in frame; the subtitles carry the words), no emblem anywhere at the plant. **Prompts:** docs/cutscenes/SHOT_PROMPTS.md is generated from the JSON (`archive_reel.py prompts`, `--check`, a Python and a GUT test), priority order per scene, every prompt = the work order's style block + content + a closing line (no text/insignia, nobody hurt, fictional people). **The tool** tools/cinema/archive_reel.py (stdlib + ffmpeg): `build` treats each raw clip (assets/user_supplied/cutscenes/<scene>/shot_NN.mp4, optional shot_NN_gore) into assets/cutscene_reels/<scene>/shot_NN.ogv (1080p) + _720.ogv: conform to the span (trim; or slow up to 1.5x then hold the last frame with a slow zoompan push), grey, a 256-entry LUT matching the clip's luma histogram to the scene's reference frame (the reference_shot clip's middle frame, else a built-in archival target), the archival curve (contrast, lifted blacks, a gain under white), sepia for 1930 and black-and-white for 1944, the Academy 1.375 gate pillarboxed in 16:9, a vignette; incremental by input hash (build.json); copies SOURCE/LICENSE. **The game clips carry no baked damage**: grain, scratches, dust, weave and flicker are drawn live by the same shader the stand-ins use, so a clip and a stand-in look like one film, and grain (which Theora compresses terribly: 3.5 MB for 3 s of 640x360) stays out of the web download. `reel` renders the finished film of a scene (build/reels/<scene>_reel.ogv + _720, Theora + Vorbis): each clip with the damage baked in (dust and scratches from random.Random(sha1 seed), weave and flicker from integer hashes of the frame number, noise with a fixed seed), a still `standin_NN.png` with a slow push or film leader where there is no clip, the cards (Russian / German in Old Standard TT, the in-engine English under it in Special Elite; a German title identical to the English is said once), the baked VO takes, the shots' sfx and the beds at the definition's times. **Determinism:** single-threaded filters, -fflags +bitexact, -flags +bitexact, -map_metadata -1; a second render is byte-identical (test_archive_reel: the build and the whole STAUB reel on testsrc clips). **The Windows ffmpeg (gyan 8.1, the PC's) writes Theora no decoder accepts** ("error in unpack_block_qpis", ffmpeg trac #8040/#8775: the 64-bit Windows libtheora encoder; a reference 3-qi stream from xiph decodes fine, so the decoder is not the problem), so the tool encodes a 1 s probe, decodes it, and falls back to the first WSL distro's ffmpeg (Ubuntu 24.04, ffmpeg 6.1: clean); REEL_FFMPEG overrides; with no working encoder `build` builds nothing and the game keeps its stand-ins. Godot's headless dummy renderer returns a blank texture, so frame content is verified with the local ffmpeg decoder (zero errors), not in Godot. **The player:** CinemaVideo is the `cinema_video` shot type: a shot's `video` (+ `video_from`, `video_graded`) or a definition's `videos` track; a VideoStreamPlayer on canvas layer 7 (over the 3D and the grade, under the film and the frame) driven by the director's clock (started at the span's offset, at the cut's speed, re-seeked on 0.3 s drift x speed), the director viewport's 3D switched off while a clip covers it; the in-engine shots under a clip keep running (lines, cards, Gore), so video and in-engine shots mix in one definition and a reel cuts straight into the next cut or gameplay. CinemaFilm is the stand-in treatment: the overlay's black is drawn on layer 8 under the film (its alpha still drives it), the native cards on 9, the shader on 29 (tone, curve, per-film-frame grain at 18 fps, scratches, dust, weave, flicker, vignette, the Academy gate), seeded per reel shot (sha1 of "<scene>/shot_NN" as the tool), only the damage over a graded clip; `film_grain` off removes the grain. CinemaArchive routes ids: `reel:<id>` the reel, `engine:<id>` the in-engine scene, a plain id the reel when cinema_reels.json `in_game` is true (it is: the campaign, the Archive and the U11 buttons now play the reels; one flag to flip back), picks the 720p clip on the web and a _gore clip only with Gore full, reads data/look/cinema_reel.json (§1, C2) when it exists for grain/vignette/contrast. CinemaReel and CinemaDirector got additive hooks only (the signals carry the plain id). **Licensing:** each clip folder has SOURCE.txt (tool: Higgsfield in the Claude app, model and dates to fill in, the owner's account) and LICENSE.txt (the plan's commercial-use terms, NOT confirmed: development and private builds only until the owner checks them); the licence gate now checks assets/user_supplied/cutscenes (the rest of the drop box stays exempt) and accepts the "Owner-generated (Higgsfield)" licence only there and in assets/cutscene_reels; the raw clips are git-ignored and never exported, the built .ogv are LFS and exported (`*.ogv` in both presets' include filter); build_web.sh / build_windows.sh run `archive_reel.py build` first. Old Standard TT (OFL, Alexey Kryukov) added for the Cyrillic cards (Stardos Stencil and Special Elite have none). **Narration:** no new ElevenLabs bake (about 1,800 characters left until 2026-12-07); every line of the three scenes already has a take; each scene has an empty `narration` slot a December bake can fill, and `report` lists any line with no take. Tests: test_cinema_reels (14), test_license_gate (+1), tools/cinema/test_archive_reel.py (10); smokes smoke_cinema_reels (stand-ins, clips, skip, subtitles, mixed into GRAVELIGHT ONE) and smoke_cinema_reels_online (STAUB's reel with clips, host + client, the vote); smoke_cinema_online takes `--cut=`. Dev launcher: "Cutscene — Lazarus Saturday / Fourth Day / Staub (reel)" (U12_BUTTONS). **Looked at:** one off-screen rendered run (C3 lock, --position -4000,-4000, silent) of STAUB's reel stand-ins through clip_cutscene --stills: the shader compiles and reads as intended (black-and-white, the Academy gate, the vignette; headless never compiles shaders); the 1080p finished STAUB reel on testsrc clips was checked frame by frame with ffmpeg (the German card, a treated shot). **Found, not mine:** at the game's speed STAUB drops 2-3 of its 12 subtitles in the in-engine scene too (overlapping lines queue in GLVO and the queue is cut; which ones depends on frame timing); the reel smoke compares the reel's count with the in-engine scene's instead of demanding all 12.
T-208, 2026-11-08: UPDATE 12 section 2, the shared look: the post stack, the material pass, darker nights, Groom Lake (branch u12-2-post; contract C2). **One post pass, under the HUD:** `gravelight/world/look/look_post.gd` (class LookPost) + `look_post.gdshader`, a CanvasLayer at layer 5 with one full-screen ColorRect that reads the screen once (canvas_item, unshaded, no mipmaps, no compute, no cull-mode games: the same on Forward+ and Compatibility / WebGL 2). It draws the edge-only lens softness (a 5-tap blur, radius growing from zero at 60% of the half-diagonal) and the edge-only chromatic aberration (2 more taps, radial, zero in the centre), the oval vignette (1.15 : 1 from 55% of the half-diagonal), luma-weighted monochrome grain (a new hash per frame, stronger in the shadows) and the projector flicker (menu). The centre 60% of the frame is one tap a pixel. The tonemap, the per-map grade (the LUT) and the fog stay the Environment's (LookGrade, T-207); volumetric fog stays gated by the Volumetric fog quality row (GLSettings `gfx_fog` turns `volumetric_fog_enabled` off whatever the grade sets; LookGrade only writes the density). Chosen over a Compositor effect because a CanvasLayer screen read works on Compatibility and costs one copy, and every map already carried one (PostFx): `gravelight/ui/postfx.gd` is now `class PostFx extends LookPost`, so every map scene got the stack with no scene edit and the cutscene HUD-hiding code (`is PostFx`) still skips it. **Data:** `LookGrade.post_params(map_id, zone)` (the json `post` block) drives the uniforms; a map with no grade or `--look=off` keeps the pre-U12 PostFx look exactly (vignette 0.42 + the cold shadow lift: `legacy`), so the before frames are the U11 game; the web profile merges `data/look/_web.json`'s new `post` block (edge_soft 0, ca 0: the browser keeps grain + vignette + flicker, one tap a pixel). **Toggles:** Settings > Video gains Vignette, Lens softness at the edges, Chromatic aberration (frame edge) beside Film grain (GLSettings `post_vignette`, `post_edge_soft`, `post_ca`, `film_grain`); everything off skips the pass. The old full-screen grain overlay (GLSettings layer 120, over the HUD) only shows where no LookPost draws its own grain (a map with no grade, the other modes). **Where it sits:** maps (PostFx, the map's map_id), the main menu (`LookPost.add_to(MenuScene, "menu", -2)`, under the fade and every menu; capture.gd keeps LookPost layers in shots), and a CinemaReel stage (inside the reel's SubViewport at layer 5, under its letterbox and cards; the host map's grade, `menu` in the theatre). **The reels stand aside:** `cinema_reel` (lazarus_saturday, fourth_day, staub) is section 3's archive treatment (the reel shader / the treated .ogv): LookPost.REEL_GRADES hides the pass there, so nothing doubles. The egg-mode tag moved from layer 4 to 9 and Mondlicht's suit HUD from 5 to 6, so the edge softness never blurs UI text. **Cost:** one screen copy + one pass at window resolution (the old PostFx pass was already one): Map 0 on Low (Forward+, RTX 5090 off-screen, perf_marks --breakdown; `--ab=no_post` added to perf_marks for the A/B) GPU 2.60 -> 2.73 ms a frame (+0.13 ms); on the Compatibility renderer (the web preset, run off-screen on the desktop instead of a web build) the A/B difference was inside the noise (17.2 vs 18.2 FPS, both CPU-bound). Estimated on the min-spec Iris Xe at 1080p: ~0.4-0.5 ms (copy + ~3.6 taps a pixel on average) on Forward+, ~0.3 ms on the web profile (1 tap), against ~0.25 ms for the old PostFx pass + grain overlay it replaces. **FPS honesty:** the in-play numbers on this PC were CPU-bound and shared with four other workers' Godot processes (Map 0 Low 43-48 FPS with scripts at 23 ms a frame, web 17-18 FPS); they show the post is flat in the A/B but cannot confirm the U09 section 2d targets; that needs the owner's frame-log run on a quiet machine. **The material pass** (`gravelight/world/look/look_materials.gd`, class LookMaterials, rules in `data/look/_materials.json`): run once per map from LookGrade.attach (after the map's children have built themselves; 14-42 ms a map) on every world material: roughness floor 0.45 / metal 0.35 (a clean plastic, glossy + flat + saturated, goes to 0.55), albedo saturation under 0.30 (0.15 for a non-accent material in the violet / red hue windows: a red curtain under a warm lamp came back out of the grade's red keep) and value 0.04-0.90, and the wear layer: a shared generated grime map (`assets/materials/textures/look_grime.png`, our own, `tools/look/make_grime.py`) as a multiply detail on every StandardMaterial3D (world-triplanar on an untextured one), the shared surface shader's `grime` raised to 0.35. Textured materials are desaturated, not repainted: the surface shader gained `albedo_saturation` (default 1.0: nothing else changes), a StandardMaterial3D gets a desaturated copy of its texture made once at load (Image.adjust_bcs, at most 1024 px, mipmapped, shared). The first try, a per-channel multiply up to x12, speckled Totenhafen's rusty barrels blue: thrown out (a multiply capped at x1.6 is only the fallback for a texture that can't be read). Skipped: characters (rigged), players / mounted guns / the armorer's vise pistol (gun-metal rules), section 5's C-47 (node names Wreck / C47, any path with c47), perk machines (the bible's readability rule: buyables keep their T-088 colours), the cartoon worlds; exempt: transparent, unshaded, emissive materials and every shader other than surface_pbr (counted by name in the report). **The audit** (`tools/look/material_audit.sh` -> `gravelight/tools_src/material_audit.tscn` per map, headless, the pass held back with `LookGrade.hold_materials`, every material read with its textures' real averages; `tools/look/material_audit.py` merges the texture fixes into the data with --write-fixes and writes `docs/look/material_audit.md`): **2,370 outliers before, 2 after** (Map 0 495 -> 1, Kaltenbrunn 372 -> 0, Totenhafen 422 -> 1, Thalbach 308 -> 0, der Kranich 224 -> 0, Groom Lake 309 -> 0, Mondlicht 240 -> 0; by kind before: rough_low 180, clean_plastic 42, albedo_sat 738, albedo_value 83, no_wear 1,327). The two left, with reasons in the report: the varnished round table and the enamel propane bottle, whose roughness maps average under the floor at scalar 1.0 (StandardMaterial3D can't lift a map past itself). **Darker nights** (the grade's new `lights` block, applied by `LookGrade.attach_scene` from MapRoot._enter_tree, before any lamp script caches its energy; the `energy` meta scaled too): ambient Map 0 0.7 -> 0.62x, Totenhafen and Thalbach 0.85 -> 0.72x, Kaltenbrunn's surface fill 0.26 -> 0.22; practical lamps x1.25 on Map 0, x1.2 on Kaltenbrunn, Totenhafen, Thalbach. Readability checked on the u05 close_zombie frame (the risen Ganger at 3.4 m in the stable, lit and separate from the floor) and u05_objects ammo_box / wall_buy_close (the box under its lamp; the prompt and the HUD crisp, no grain or blur on them). **Groom Lake (the lead's send-back: lavender, not bleached desert):** the cause was the desert sky's dawn defaults (a violet Belt of Venus `belt`, a blue zenith and band) and a blue-lavender ambient (0.5, 0.56, 0.74), with the crater's violet lamps (range 10-20 m) on top; not the grade. Fixed in data, not by greying violet: the grade's new `sky` block sets the desert sky shader's zenith / upper / band / belt / ground to dust browns, the ambient to #7a6c5c, the volumetric emission to #060504, and a `lights` rule shrinks the violet lamps (hue 268 +-30: range x0.45, energy x0.8, fog x0.3), so the violet stays on the Nachtglas (docs/look/post/map04c_crater_after: the salt dust brown, one violet core). **The red keep narrowed** (T-207's LUT, every map but Mondlicht): 0 +-18 -> 0 +-12 degrees: warm lamps at hue 17-23 were half kept and painted reddish halos once the rest went grey (ART_BIBLE section 6 updated). **Captures:** `tools/look/post_frames.sh` (the u12_look set + the new u12_post set: an interior or second view per map and act, Groom Lake's crater, plus the readability frames; `--look=off` for before), filed by `tools/look/post_frames.py` into docs/look/post/<name>_before|after.jpg (<= 400 KB, the folder .gdignored) + sheet.jpg, printing each frame's mean luma and saturated / violet share. Seen and left for the lead: the taproom keeps a faint pink rim on the wall round the windows (cold moon spill meeting the warm lamps: lighting, not the post); Mondlicht's concourse is ~60% violet pixels after the grade (its lamps are violet by design; the bible's 25% is for the hall); one after-capture run crashed once on the Map 0 -> Kaltenbrunn switch (0xC0000374 after the engine's "indexing did not unpair geometries from light" errors), the rerun of the same cameras was clean; no exit 127 seen. **Tests:** gravelight/tests/unit/test_look_post.gd (the post's uniforms per map, each switch, the web preset, the reel grade, the legacy look, under the HUD and owning the grain; the lights block once and the violet rule; Groom Lake's sky / ambient / the darker nights; the material rules, the fix once, snow keeps no dirt, the texture fix and the capped multiply, the audit's collect / skip / apply). Presentation only: no gameplay number, timing, hitbox, navmesh or prompt touched.

T-214, 2026-11-08: UPDATE 12, the blind look review's findings (5/10 before, 7/10 after T-207/T-208) fixed (branch u12-7-lookfix). Data first (data/look/*.json, _materials.json); the code only grew what the data needed. **New data keys** (LookGrade, contract C2): a `keep` entry takes `sat` [from, full] and `val` [from, full] (how saturated / how bright a colour must be to keep its accent: blood red is past 0.6-0.85, a warm-lit board in the filmic toe is not; a lit tube is violet, a floor washed by its fill is not); `suppress` [{hue, width, amount, max_sat}] greys a hue band whatever `mono` leaves (the pink / salmon / lavender that orange lamps plus the blue moon sum to on a pale wall: the recurring cause behind findings 3, 4, 7 and 9); `env` sets any Environment property by name (sdfgi_energy, ssil_intensity); `sky` also sets a ProceduralSkyMaterial's properties; the `lights` rules match by `type` (omni / spot / directional: the moon only by a rule that names it), `name`, `min_range` / `max_range`, `box` (map frame), and do `sat_scale`, `color` and `pool` (a small light of the old colour left under a light whose fill went neutral); a rule's or the practical scale now also scales a FlickerLight's / HumPulse's `base_energy` (T-208's practical_scale never reached the flicker lamps: their scripts reset the energy every frame); `shader_params` [{shader, node, params}] sets a ShaderMaterial's uniforms on the map (and on any mesh that enters the map later: the living world's extra searchlights); `nodes` [{path, scale, material}] reshapes a far set piece; `zones` with `boxes` now follow the camera on any map without its own zone logic (gravelight/world/look/look_zones.gd, class LookZones: fog density / volumetric / fog colour / ambient scale / exposure blend in 0.4 s inside a box and back out; it writes only while it blends). The material pass (`_materials.json`): the grime layer has a `strength` (a flat detail mask; Godot's detail_mask scales the multiply) and a size `tile_m`, per `detail_classes` (a pale flat colour 0.28 at 1.2 m, concrete / steel / plaster / panel 0.38 at 1.4 m, the rest 0.6 at 1.6 m; was full strength at 2.5 m), a textured material wears on its own UV2 triplanar (the grime was stretched over its UV tile: 2-5 m blotches), `maps.<map_id>` merges per-map rules over the shared ones (LookMaterials.rules_for; the audit uses them too), `emission` / `accent_caps` / `lit_smoke` act on the emissive / unshaded materials the pass otherwise leaves alone, GPUParticles3D draw passes are collected, and a code-built NoiseTexture2D (unreadable at load, so it was never desaturated) gets a desaturated copy of its colour ramp. **Per finding** (frames docs/look/post/*_after.jpg, sheet.jpg; violet share measured with the new tools/look/violet_share.py, the bible's measure = pixels past saturation 0.5 in hue 240-300): (1) **Mondlicht concourse** 68.6% violet -> 0.7% (4.0% faintly tinted): the ambient #24262b instead of violet (0.17, 0.15, 0.26), SDFGI 2.8 -> 1.2 and SSIL 1.2 -> 0.6 (the GI bounced the violet over every wall), the long violet fills (range >= 12 m: the B strips, the hum pulses, the F room) go neutral (sat x0.12) and leave a 3.2 m violet pool each, the shorter violet lamps reach 0.55x with less fog, the keep only holds a violet past saturation 0.5-0.75 and value 0.3-0.5 (a dark floor washed violet goes grey; the tubes' glow and the Crucible keep theirs); the hall reads hard black and white. (2) **Mondlicht hall**: grime 0.14 at 1.0 m on Mondlicht (`maps.map04d_mondlicht`): the walls and ceiling are clean concrete, no camouflage; the airlock's violet no longer pools on the floor (range 0.55x, keep needs brightness): 0.0% violet. (3) **Barracks**: the "floral" was the shared grime map at full strength on the untextured tan bunk boards, pushed pink by the filmic toe (a dark warm orange turns red) and the old red keep (any red past saturation 0.3); now the pale-flat grime class, the red keep needs saturation 0.6+, Kaltenbrunn's mono 0.4 -> 0.52 plus the pink suppress; the room: the Lamp_ lights in the barracks box x2.0 energy, x1.3 range (and the flicker lamps now take the practical scale): far bunks, floor and lockers read; the medic's white apron is his look (people are skipped by the pass) and no longer glares in a black room. (4) **Taproom**: the halo was the blue MoonSpill spots (volumetric fog x3.5) through the windows meeting the warm WindowSpill spots on the plaster (blue + orange = pink), kept by the red keep; now the moon spots sat x0.45 / fog x0.2, the warm window spots fog x0.35, the pink suppress, the curtains desaturated (their code-built noise texture had never been fixed); interior haze: zone `inn` (taproom, rooms, passage, stable, cellar) fog 0.022 -> 0.006, volumetric 0.03 -> 0.008: the fireplace and far wall read. (5) **Groom Lake**: mono 0.6 -> 0.7, the split tone #3e352b -> #dcd6cc at 0.3 (was #4a3d30 -> #ece0c8 at 0.5), exposure 1.7 -> 1.35, contrast 1.1, the ridges' haze (desert_ridge.gdshader, set by `shader_params`) dust brown instead of blue (0.5, 0.56, 0.7): steel and the hull stay grey-brown, the mountains brown-grey, the hex salt a taupe grey, no longer near-white (the floor's mean luma 89 -> 79 of 255). (6) **Thalbach**: ochre and soot: the procedural sky to soot browns, fog #4a3a26 / volumetric albedo #c9ad80 / emission #0b0804, the split tone #241c12 -> #d6aa62 at 0.55, the ambient #4f4130 (0.82x; the test pins a darker night than T-207's), the moon desaturated, the warm practicals recoloured sodium #ffb04a x1.3, the cloud deck soot-brown with dim searchlight spots, the beams sodium-grey; the bugs: the **black rectangle** is the air-raid cellar's blockhouse (CellarE wall + its moon shadow on the yard) in a night with no fill: with the warmer, brighter ambient it reads as a wall with sandbags (not geometry missing); the **black box props** are the laundry on the washing lines, their backs to the moon (dark cloth, textured, now visible as cloth); the **blown-out tube** (crater frame) is **not fixed**: bisected with throwaway grades it disappears only with ThalbachSky's Ridges ring hidden, and it is none of the beams, the cloud spots, the false dawn, an emissive material (the works' cold tubes went x9 -> x2.7 anyway), a light or SSR; it was in the U11 frame too. Left for the owner's eye / a later pass. (7) **Kaltenbrunn**: the pylon cable (Gameplay/Pylon_A, unshaded #8c40ff) capped at saturation 0.5 / value 0.85 (`accent_caps`, back to the pre-U12 pastel); the salmon plaster was warm practical + moon light on a neutral grey noise plaster, kept by the red keep: the sat-gated red keep, mono 0.52 and the pink / salmon suppress grey it; the "cloud layer's hard polygon edge" is the far RidgeBand1 (a 170 m ridge 330 m off filled the sky to the frame top, its fogged face the sky's colour, so only its jagged crest read, as a cloud): scaled to 0.6 height (`nodes`), now a mountain line under the sky. (8) **Der Kranich**: the violet strips' emission x0.5 (`maps.map04b_kranich.emission`) and the violet lamp energy x0.5 / range x0.6: 2.2% violet. (9) **Map 0 / C-47** (docs/look/c47/after_*.jpg re-shot, layout and colliders untouched): the lavender snow was the warm split-tone highlights (#d9c9a8) on blue moonlight plus the fire: highlights #c9d0d6 / shadows #1a2a3a, the moon desaturated (x0.5), a lavender suppress band (low saturation only): snow saturation 0.27-0.31 -> 0.12-0.17, cold grey; the wings' paper-white undersides were the skin shader's flat snow (#d1d9eb, no variation): snow_tint 0.70 / 0.73 / 0.78 with a drift noise; the cabin's white triangles: c47_art now turns the 49 fuselage faces (of 14,492) whose vertex normals ran against their winding (C47Art.fix_flipped_normals; 1 degenerate left), and the slivers seen through the tear are now grey (peak 171 -> 141 of 255), but they are still there and their source is not pinned down (rays find only skin faces whose colours should char them dark): flagged for the C-47 owner; the serial (field_se) is still small at that distance (minor, not touched: it is the decal atlas's size). (10) **Totenhafen cannery**: the "ghost" was the stove barrel's smoke column (Props.smoke: an unshaded dark-grey alpha billboard) reading pale against an unlit hall; `lit_smoke` makes Props.smoke materials take the scene's light (per-vertex), so smoke in a dark hall is dark and over the wreck fire it glows orange; the cannery's west moon shafts fog x0.35. **Readability** (u05 close_zombie, u05_objects ammo_box / wall_buy_close / perk_dark re-shot): the risen Ganger, the boxes, the wall buy and its prompt read; the menu untouched (B&W). **Material audit** (tools/look/material_audit.sh, per-map rules now): 2,369 outliers before, 2 after (unchanged: the varnished table and the propane bottle). **Tools**: tools/look/post_frames.sh takes `only=` (re-shoot a few cameras; the sheet is rebuilt from every pair in the folder), tools/look/probe.sh + gravelight/tools_src/look_probe.gd (headless: what mesh / material / face is under a capture pixel, `--emissive` lists every glow and light in the frame, `--normals` counts faces whose normals run against their winding, `--c47=<view>`), tools/look/violet_share.py. **Seen**: one capture run crashed silently on Map 0's establishing camera (the T-208 "indexing did not unpair geometries" note); the rerun was clean. A stray run of another worker's leftover scratch script (the shared session scratchpad) rewrote tools/c47/build_c47.py and data/gravelight/c47_wreck.json in the u12-5-c47 worktree with byte-identical content (both match master; only the mtimes changed). **Tests**: gravelight/tests/unit/test_look_fix.gd (the sat-gated red keep, the pink / lavender suppress, Mondlicht's strong-violet-only keep, the light rules' neutral fill + pool, type / name / box / base energy, the inn zone, the grime classes and per-map rules, the special material rules, shader params and node tweaks). Presentation only: no gameplay number, timing, hitbox, navmesh or prompt touched (the pylon cable keeps its place and readability; only its colour is capped).

T-215, 2026-11-08, UPDATE 12 FINISH: every section merged (T-207 bible + grades, T-208 post stack + materials, T-209 archive reels, T-210 mocap + lip sync, T-211 the C-47, T-212 aircraft, T-213 VO upkeep, T-214 the blind review's fixes). The lead ran as Opus 5.5 (the work order asked for Fable 5.1; a session can't change its own model), so the look judging went to Fable workers and the blind review to a fresh Fable reviewer (5/10 before, 7/10 after; its findings fixed in T-214). Off-screen rendered runs were allowed for captures (the owner away, the work order needs captures) under the U12_CONTRACTS C3 GPU lock. Added the dev launcher's last three U12 buttons (Look test, Map 0 crash intro, Map 0 walk the wreck: no zombies via the session's egg_mode "none", restored after the match takes it). Full GUT suite 2074/2075 (1 risky, no asserts), smokes 95/97 (letzte_fahrt's 20-min cap and smoke_marks' pause-map check fail as before; bot_loco, lf_start, milestone_r5 failed once under 3-shard load and passed alone). Windows build rebuilt; it boots to the menu. Open: FPS targets unconfirmed (every run was CPU-loaded); rare heap-corruption crashes in capture runs after "indexing did not unpair geometries from light" errors; one Godot exit hang in a test run; a blown-out streak in Thalbach's sky ring; white slivers through the C-47 cabin tear.

T-216, 2026-11-08, the owner: "campaign just crashes when I try to launch it"; the code review of 88c0bc21 (the crash probe + run/flush_stdout_on_print). Findings: the probe's --menu run never quit (two heartbeats shared one counter: one heartbeat now, process_frame, PASS at --seconds in every mode); the probe wrote --set overrides into the owner's live settings (it now uses copies of both the settings and the profile, stale copies removed, a failed copy reported); the --set parser crashed on a missing colon and kept numbers as text (now typed as the setting's own type, graphics_preset through set_preset, bad input ignored with a line); the direct path hard-coded map00 and skipped the survivor (now Campaign.first_map() and GameState.character_id, --char=); the start sequence was a hand copy (MenuCharacter.prepare_match is now shared by the menu and the probe); the header now says --audio-driver Dummy (CLAUDE.md), with the real driver only with the owner's OK. run/flush_stdout_on_print stays: the reviewer was right that Play.bat (the editor binary, debug feature) already flushed through the .debug default, but the 09:43 log that stopped after 5 lines came from the exported exe (no editor-only lines), a release build, which did not flush; that exe (06:57) predates the setting, so it was rebuilt. Release exes refuse a scene path on the command line, so GLSettings starts the probe on `-- --probe-campaign` (like --frame-log). In the rebuilt exe, headless with copies of the owner's settings (ultra, 3 bots, Bot AI auto -> small, gemma3:4b): the campaign start loaded Map 0 and was alive at 150 s with no errors; the exe then exited with code 127 after its PASS line (a possible crash at shutdown, not followed up). The cost is a user-space flush per print (not a disk sync) at a few lines a second. Cause of the crash: not reproduced. The probe's campaign start ran 213 s alive in the editor binary with the owner's settings; the owner's 11:46 session ran 2,200+ s, hit "indexing did not unpair geometries from light" in map_root._restart (game over -> Restart -> reload_current_scene) and later stopped with no error (the known heap-corruption lead, T-208/T-214). Also found: Ollama held gemma3:27b in 17 GB of video memory with a 32k context for 30 minutes after the game closed (keep_alive 30m), 24 of the 5090's 32 GB in use with no game running; fixed in T-217.

T-217, 2026-11-08, the owner: "a lower end model or a setting for different local AIs ... quicker decisions ... train it on the data we already have ... this needs to work for all types of computers". The planning bot gets a brain setting (bot_ai_brain: auto / builtin / small / small_cpu / large; the squad row's "Bot AI" button cycles it with Off; Settings > Gameplay has the same row). Built-in needs nothing (no Ollama, no GPU) and is the fallback of every model brain; the Ollama tiers are bots.json local_ai.brains (small gemma3:4b first, small_cpu gemma3:1b with num_gpu 0, large gemma3:27b), all with num_ctx 2048; keep_alive 2m; auto = small, small_cpu on a machine whose first-launch preset is low, builtin on the web. Measured with the GPU idle: 1b 0.36 s, 4b 0.53 s / 2.9 GB, 27b 0.78 s / 17 GB (1.7 s median in the owner's matches), 1b on the CPU 1.75 s, 4b on the CPU 2.8 s. gemma3:1b was pulled on the owner's PC. A planner with one option no longer asks the model. "Train on the data we have": the old logs only held ~260 changed picks with no option lists (82 % rebuild boards), not enough to train on, so every model answer now logs a full row (user://bot_ai/decisions.jsonl) and tools/bot_ai/train_planner.py fits the built-in brain's weights (score scale, per-kind bias, distance, keep-current bonus; a softmax choice model, written only when it beats the plain scores on held-out rows). Trained on 483 rows from two headless bots3 soaks with the 27B as teacher (ai_soak --local-ai=large): held-out agreement 87 % plain -> 96 % trained. Trained built-in vs the 27B in 2 + 2 bots3 soaks to round 12: both reach round 13 every run, power thrown and doors opened by both, stuck events 54-58 vs 94-101. Fine-tuning the LLM itself was not done: a few hundred rows of mostly one kind would not change a model, and the built-in brain already copies the 27B's picks. Map 0 only so far (docs/BOT_AI.md section 16).

T-218, 2026-11-08, the owner: "Menus & UI. Polish the Claude Zombies main menu, HUD, settings and perk icons" (focus asked: main menu, Settings, HUD; rendered runs OK'd, all done under WSL + Xvfb so nothing opened on the owner's screen). Before / after sets: morning/screenshots/u13_ui_before and u13_ui_after (shots_menus, shots_hud, shots_squad_hud, shots_pause; git-ignored). Main menu: the CLAUDE ZOMBIES lockup is left-aligned with its letters on the column's rule (it was centred over the wider TERMS & CONDITIONS line, so title, tagline and menu started at three x positions), the wordmark 140 -> 184 px, the footer keys switch to A / B with a controller (hint_text), the version bottom right. Settings: the tab bar moved out of the scroll into MenuScreen.header (new, empty for other pages) so it never scrolls away; the open tab is gold with an underline and keeps the focus (rebuild's focus_first used to land on the first control in the content, the Gameplay tab, so Gameplay looked selected on every tab); Q / E switch tabs as well as LB / RB, shown at both ends of the bar; every tab grouped under section headings (Gameplay: Camera and aim, Rules and practice, AI teammates, Homage, M1911, Minimap); sliders print their value (MenuKit.slider fmt: pct / x / deg / a format; row() adds the readout); rows take 72 % of the width (no 1,400 px sliders); on/off settings are switch_row (label in the label column, a drawn switch at the end of the control column; off used to be a lone dot); help lines 17 px, lighter, indented (MenuKit.help); Controls has column headers and the pad's own names (LB, RT, Left stick up, L3: MenuSettings.pad_name; prompts elsewhere unchanged). Accessibility: Subtitle size (normal / large / largest) and Subtitle background (off / light / dark band), both off-by-default settings so the look is unchanged until asked.
